\input{sections/05c_secondary_comparisons}
\fi


 succeeded in 204ms:
\subsection{The bounded sliding-congruence lemma}

The separation estimate used in the sliding argument is isolated first.  In
particular, the integer multiple and the error term are both retained.

\begin{lemma}[Nearest-multiple separation]
\label{lem:nearest-multiple-separation}
Let $Q_0,Q_1,\ldots$ satisfy either \eqref{eq:qminus} or \eqref{eq:qplus},
write $s=1$ or $s=-1$ accordingly, and put
\[
d=\begin{cases}a,&s=1,\\a-1,&s=-1.\end{cases}
\]
Then, for $r\ge4$ and $1\le |e|\le d$,
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{dist}(beQ_{r-1},Q_r\mathbb Z)\ge Q_{r-2}.
\label{eq:quadratic-separation}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Distance is unchanged by replacing $e$ with $-e$, so assume $1\le e\le d$.
Set
\[
R_r:=\frac{Q_{r-1}}{Q_{r-2}}.
\]
The recurrence gives $R_{r+1}=a+sb/R_r$.  Its initial values and the parameter
ranges imply, by induction,
\begin{align}
a+\frac{b}{a+1}&\le R_r<a+\frac ba &&(s=1,\ r\ge3),
\label{eq:ratio-plus}\\
a-\frac{b}{a-1}&<R_r\le a-\frac ba &&(s=-1,\ r\ge3).
\label{eq:ratio-minus}
\end{align}
For completeness, in the positive-recurrence case the map
$x\mapsto a+b/x$ reverses order, sends
$[a+b/(a+1),a+b/a)$ into itself, and $R_3=a+b/(a+1)$.
In the negative-recurrence case $x\mapsto a-b/x$ preserves order, the interval
in \eqref{eq:ratio-minus} lies in $(a-1,a)$, and $R_3=a-b/a$.
These observations prove the two inductions, including the stated strict
endpoints.

We shall also use the following sharpened bounds, obtained by applying the
same recurrence once more:
\begin{align*}
L_+&:=a+\frac{b}{a+b/(a+b/(a+1))},&
U_+&:=a+\frac{b}{a+b/(a+1)},\\
L_-&:=a-\frac{b}{a-1},&
U_-&:=a-\frac{b}{a-b/a}.
\end{align*}
Thus
\begin{equation}
L_+\le R_r\le U_+\quad(s=1,r\ge4),
\qquad
L_-<R_r\le U_-\quad(s=-1,r\ge4).
\label{eq:sharpened-ratio-bounds}
\end{equation}
The first assertion follows because the order-reversing map interchanges the
endpoints $R_4=U_+$ and $R_5=L_+$ and maps $[L_+,U_+]$ into itself.  The
second follows because $R_3=a-b/a$, $R_4=U_-$, and the increasing map sends
$(L_-,U_-]$ into itself.

Choose a nearest integer $k$ to
\[
z:=\frac{beQ_{r-1}}{Q_r}=\frac{be}{R_{r+1}}.
\]
The lower bounds $R_{r+1}>a$ for $s=1$ and $R_{r+1}>a-1$ for $s=-1$ give
\[
0<z<b.
\]
Indeed $e\le a$ in the first case and $e\le a-1$ in the second.  Hence every
nearest integer satisfies
\begin{equation}
0\le k\le b.
\label{eq:nearest-k-bound}
\end{equation}
Put $h:=be-ak$.  The exact error, with no omitted remainder, is
\begin{align}
E&:=beQ_{r-1}-kQ_r\notag\\
 &=(be-ak)Q_{r-1}-skbQ_{r-2}
   =(hR_r-skb)Q_{r-2}.
\label{eq:nearest-error}
\end{align}

\paragraph{The positive recurrence.}
Put
\[
S:=a^2+a+b,
\qquad
T:=a^3+a^2+2ab+b=aS+b(a+1).
\]
Both denominators are positive for every $a\ge b\ge1$, and reduction of the
nested fractions gives
\begin{equation}
U_+-a=\frac{b(a+1)}S,
\qquad
L_+-a=\frac{bS}T.
\label{eq:positive-offsets}
\end{equation}
We first record the bounded integer exclusion for the cancelling sign.  For
$1\le e\le a$, $0\le k\le b$, and $h=be-ak$,
\begin{equation}
h>0\Longrightarrow
  hL_+-kb\ge1\quad\hbox{or}\quad kb-hU_+\ge1.
\label{eq:endpoint-plus}
\end{equation}
Write $j=kb-ah$.  Since $h=be-ak$, solving for $(e,k)$ gives
\[
k=\frac{ah+j}{b},\qquad
e=\frac{(a^2+b)h+aj}{b^2}.
\]
Thus the two displayed numerators are divisible by $b$ and $b^2$,
respectively.  We give the remaining Euclidean divisions explicitly.  Write
\[
be=aq+r,\qquad qb=ap+t,\qquad 0\le r,t<a.
\]
Because $be\le ab$, one has $0\le q\le b$; if $r>0$, then $q\le b-1$.
These statements use no coprimality of $a$ and $b$.
If $k\le q-1$, then $h\ge a+r$ and
\[
hL_+-kb>(a+r)a-(b-1)b
\ge a^2-b(b-1)\ge a\ge1.
\]
If $k\ge q+1$, then $h<0$, so this case is not in the hypothesis of
\eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}.  Hence only $k=q$ remains, and then $h=r$.
Now $j=a(p-r)+t$.  The cases $p<r$ and $p>r$ give, respectively,
$hL_+-kb\ge1$ and $kb-hU_+\ge1$.  Explicitly, when $p<r$,
\[
rL_+-qb=a(r-p)-t+r(L_+-a)>a-t\ge1,
\]
whereas, when $p>r$, the bounds $0<U_+-a<b/a$ and $r<a$ give
\[
qb-rU_+=a(p-r)+t-r(U_+-a)
>a-\frac{rb}{a}\ge a-b+\frac ba\ge1.
\]
When $p=r$, put $w=rb-at$.  The two division identities give
\begin{equation}
b^2e=r(a^2+b)+at,\qquad w=r(a^2+2b)-b^2e.
\label{eq:plus-terminal-divisions}
\end{equation}
The integer $w$ is nonzero.  To verify this without a hidden coprimality
assumption, suppose $w=0$ and write $a=gA$, $b=gB$ with
$\gcd(A,B)=1$.  From $at=rb$ one obtains $r=AR$ and $t=BR$ with
$0<R<g$.  Reducing $be=aq+r$ and $qb=ar+t$ modulo $g$ gives
$g\mid AR$ and $g\mid BR$.  Bezout's identity gives $g\mid R$, a
contradiction.

It remains to audit the signs of $w$.  Make the third Euclidean division
\begin{equation}
rb=au+v,\qquad 0\le v<a.
\label{eq:positive-third-division}
\end{equation}
Thus $w=a(u-t)+v$.  Reversing the three divisions
\[
r=be-aq,\qquad t=qb-ar,\qquad v=rb-au
\]
and retaining the ranges $1\le e\le a$, $0\le q\le b$, and $0<r<a$
gives the complete boundary sign audit
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{sign}&\text{quotient case}&\text{remainder conclusion}\\ \hline
w>0&u-t\ge2&\text{no extra inequality}\cr
w>0&u-t=1&av\ge b(t+1)\cr
w<0&t-u\ge2&\text{no extra inequality}\cr
w<0&t-u=1&b(t-1)\ge v(a+1).
\end{array}
\label{eq:positive-terminal-sign-table}
\end{equation}
No other quotient case has the indicated sign.  Here is the integer check
behind the two boundary rows.  Starting with $u=t+1$, or with $u=t-1$,
substitute successively in the reverse divisions
\[
r=\frac{au+v}{b},\qquad
q=\frac{ar+t}{b},\qquad
e=\frac{aq+r}{b}.
\]
After multiplication by the positive denominators $b,b^2,b^3$, respectively,
the failed inequalities $av<b(t+1)$ and $b(t-1)<v(a+1)$ leave, in order,
either $r\ge a$, $q>b$, or a nonintegral final quotient.  These contradict
the displayed ranges.  If equality is met before the last division, write
$a=gA$, $b=gB$ with $(A,B)=1$; the residual quotient is strictly between
$0$ and $g$, while the three divisibilities and Bezout's identity force it
to be divisible by $g$.  This is the same non-coprime endpoint exclusion as
for $w=0$, and shows that the four rows are exhaustive.

We now clear the two endpoint inequalities line by line.  Define
\begin{align*}
\Delta_L&:=rbS-(t+1)T
          =T\{r(L_+-a)-t-1\},\\
\Delta_U&:=tS-rb(a+1)-S
          =S\{t-r(U_+-a)-1\}.
\end{align*}
Using $T=aS+b(a+1)$ and $w=a(u-t)+v$ gives the exact identities
\begin{align}
\Delta_L&=wS-T-tb(a+1),
\label{eq:positive-terminal-left-clear}\\
\Delta_U&=tb+(-w)(a+1)-S.
\label{eq:positive-terminal-right-clear}
\end{align}
If $w>0$ and $u-t\ge2$, then $w\ge2a$ and $t\le a-1$, so
\[
\Delta_L
\ge2aS-T-(a-1)b(a+1)
=a^2(a-b+1)>0.
\]
If $w>0$ and $u-t=1$, then the second row of
\eqref{eq:positive-terminal-sign-table} and $S>a(a+1)$ give
\[
\Delta_L=vS-b(a+1)(t+1)\ge0.
\]
Thus $r(L_+-a)-t\ge1$ in both positive-$w$ subcases.

If $w<0$ and $t-u\ge2$, then $-w\ge2a-(a-1)=a+1$, whence
\[
\Delta_U\ge(a+1)^2-S=a+1-b\ge1.
\]
If $w<0$ and $t-u=1$, the last row of the sign table gives exactly
\[
\Delta_U=b(t-1)-v(a+1)\ge0.
\]
Thus $t-r(U_+-a)\ge1$ in both negative-$w$ subcases.  Since $w\ne0$,
the branches exhaust $p=r$ and prove \eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}.

\paragraph{The negative recurrence.}
The cancelling sign here has a norm proof.  Let $\beta$ and $\alpha$ be the
roots of $x^2-ax+b$, with $0<\alpha<1<\beta$, and put
$g=-h=ak-be>0$.  The closed formula for the recurrence gives
\[
R_r=\frac{\beta^r-\alpha^r}{\beta^{r-1}-\alpha^{r-1}}>\beta,
\]
while \eqref{eq:sharpened-ratio-bounds} gives $R_r\le U_-$.  Consider the
nonzero integral norm
\[
\nu:=k^2-aek+be^2=(k-e\alpha)(k-e\beta).
\]
Here $k\le b\le a-2<\beta\le e\beta$, so the second factor is negative.
Neither factor vanishes because $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are irrational and
$e>0$.  Hence $\nu\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$.

If $\nu<0$, then $k-e\alpha>0$, and $b=\alpha\beta$ and
$\beta^2=a\beta-b$ give
\[
g\beta-kb=\beta^2(k-e\alpha)
=\frac{\beta^2|\nu|}{e\beta-k}>1;
\]
indeed its denominator is positive and
\[
0<e\beta-k<e\beta\le(a-1)\beta<\beta^2.
\]
This remains strict at $e=a-1$.  Since $R_r>\beta$, it follows that
$gR_r-kb>1$.

Suppose $\nu>0$ and set $\delta=e\alpha-k>0$.  Then
\[
g=e\alpha^2-a\delta>0,
\qquad
\nu=\delta\{e(\beta-\alpha)+\delta\}\ge1.
\]
In particular
$0<\delta<e\alpha^2/a\le(a-1)\alpha^2/a<1$.  Put $D=a^2-b$ and
$H=(a-1)(\beta-\alpha)+1$.  Since $e\le a-1$, the norm identity gives
$\delta>H^{-1}$.  To see the strict endpoint, use $0<\delta<1$ to obtain
\[
e(\beta-\alpha)+\delta
<(a-1)(\beta-\alpha)+1=H;
\]
then $1\le\nu=\delta\{e(\beta-\alpha)+\delta\}$ gives
$\delta>H^{-1}$.  Every denominator used below is positive:
\[
D=a^2-b\ge a^2-a+2>0,
\qquad H>1,
\qquad \alpha>0,\quad \beta>0.
\]
Moreover
\[
\alpha-\frac{ab}{D}=\frac{\alpha^3}{D},
\]
and therefore
\begin{align*}
kb-gU_-
&=\beta^2\delta-\frac{g\alpha^3}{D}\\
&=\delta\left(\beta^2+\frac{a\alpha^3}{D}\right)
  -\frac{e\alpha^5}{D}\\
&>\frac{\beta^2}{H}-\frac{(a-1)\alpha^5}{D}>1.
\end{align*}
For the final inequality, write $a=\alpha+\beta$ and $b=\alpha\beta$.
Direct expansion gives
\[
D-H=2\alpha^2+\alpha(\beta-1)+(\beta-1)>0
\]
and
\[
\beta^2-H=\beta-1-\alpha(1-\alpha)>(a-1)\alpha^5.
\]
Indeed $\alpha^5<\alpha$, while $b\le a-2$ is equivalent to
$\beta(1-\alpha)\ge2-\alpha>1$.  Consequently
\begin{align*}
\beta^2-H
&=\beta-1-\alpha(1-\alpha)\\
&=(a-1)\alpha+\{\beta(1-\alpha)-1\}\\
&>(a-1)\alpha>(a-1)\alpha^5.
\end{align*}
At the boundary $b=a-2$ one has
$\beta(1-\alpha)=2-\alpha>1$, so the first strict margin is not lost.
Since $D>H>0$, division gives
\[
\frac{\beta^2-H}{H}
>\frac{(a-1)\alpha^5}{H}
>\frac{(a-1)\alpha^5}{D}.
\]
This is equivalent to
$\beta^2/H-(a-1)\alpha^5/D>1$, and completes every step in the chain
to $kb-gU_->1$, including $e=a-1$.  Since $R_r\le U_-$, this case gives
$kb-gR_r>1$.

We now cover all signs explicitly.  If $s=1$ and $h<0$, then
\[
|hR_r-kb|=(-h)R_r+kb>1.
\]
If $h=0$, then $k\ge1$ (because $e\ge1$), and
$|hR_r-kb|=kb\ge1$.  If $h>0$, the two alternatives in
\eqref{eq:endpoint-plus}, together with
$L_+\le R_r\le U_+$, give $|hR_r-kb|\ge1$.

If $s=-1$ and $h>0$, then $hR_r+kb>1$.  If $h=0$, again $k\ge1$ and the
modulus is $kb\ge1$.  The case $h<0$ was proved directly above.
Thus in both chambers
\[
|hR_r-skb|\ge1.
\]

\paragraph{Closure of the distance estimate.}
For the nearest integer $k$ chosen above, \eqref{eq:nearest-error} is the
exact identity
\[
E=beQ_{r-1}-kQ_r=(hR_r-skb)Q_{r-2}.
\]
Since $Q_{r-2}>0$, the chamberwise estimate gives $|E|\ge Q_{r-2}$.
Finally, $kQ_r\in Q_r\mathbb Z$ and $k$ was chosen nearest to
$beQ_{r-1}/Q_r$, so
\[
\operatorname{dist}(beQ_{r-1},Q_r\mathbb Z)
=|beQ_{r-1}-kQ_r|=|E|\ge Q_{r-2}.
\]
This is \eqref{eq:quadratic-separation}.
\end{proof}

The following sliding lemma is the arithmetic core of the block decoder.

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic bounded sliding congruences]
\label{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences}
Take either parameter chamber in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}, let $d=\lfloor\beta\rfloor$, and
let $(Q_j)$ be \eqref{eq:qminus} or \eqref{eq:qplus}.  If $m\ge3$ and
$e_0,\ldots,e_{2m-2}\in[-d,d]\cap\mathbb Z$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_j e_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}
\qquad(0\le t<m),\label{eq:sliding-kernel}
\end{equation}
then $e_0=\cdots=e_{2m-2}=0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $s=1$ in the negative chamber and $s=-1$ in the positive chamber, so
$Q_{j+2}=aQ_{j+1}+sbQ_j$, and put
$\delta=Q_1-a$; hence $(d,\delta)=(a,1)$ for $s=1$ and
$(d,\delta)=(a-1,0)$ for $s=-1$.  Subtracting $a$ times the congruence at
$t+1$ and $sb$ times that at $t+2$ from the congruence at $t$ cancels every
interior coefficient and gives
\begin{equation}
e_t+\delta e_{t+1}-sbQ_{m-1}e_{t+m+1}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\label{eq:quadratic-elimination}
\end{equation}

Suppose first that $m\ge4$.  The small part of
\eqref{eq:quadratic-elimination} has modulus at most
$d(1+\delta)$.  On the other hand,
\[
Q_{m-2}\ge Q_2=
\begin{cases}
a^2+a+b>2a=d(1+\delta),&s=1,\\
a^2-b>a-1=d(1+\delta),&s=-1.
\end{cases}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:nearest-multiple-separation} therefore gives a strictly larger
distance whenever $e_{t+m+1}\ne0$.  Hence
$e_{t+m+1}=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  Thus
$e_{m+1}=\cdots=e_{2m-2}=0$.  The final original congruence reduces to
\[
e_{m-1}+Q_1e_m\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\]
Its left side has modulus at most $d(1+Q_1)<Q_m$ and $Q_1>d$, so both
digits vanish.  Back substitution in the preceding congruences then gives
$e_{m-2},\ldots,e_0=0$ successively.

It remains to check $m=3$, where endpoint equality in the separation bound
can occur.  Here each integer sum in \eqref{eq:sliding-kernel} has modulus
less than $2Q_3$, so it is $k_tQ_3$ with $k_t\in\{-1,0,1\}$.  Substituting
the three values of $t$ gives
\begin{equation}
e_t+Q_1e_{t+1}+Q_2e_{t+2}=k_tQ_3,
\qquad t=0,1,2.
\label{eq:quadratic-m3-equalities}
\end{equation}
We now eliminate the two conjugate signs separately.

Suppose first that $s=1$.  Thus $d=a$ and
\[
(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3)
=(a+1,a^2+a+b,a^3+a^2+2ab+b).
\]
Consider one equation $x+Q_1y+Q_2z=kQ_3$ occurring in
\eqref{eq:quadratic-m3-equalities}.  By symmetry it suffices to treat
$k=0,1$.  If $k=0$, then $|z|\ge2$ is impossible because
$2Q_2>a(1+Q_1)$.  The choice $z=0$ gives $x=y=0$, while $z=1$ gives
\[
x+b+(a+1)(y+a)=0.
\]
Since $y+a\ge0$ and $-(x+b)\le a-b<a+1$, this forces
$(x,y,z)=(-b,-a,1)$; the case $z=-1$ is its negative.  If $k=1$ and
$z\le a-1$, then
\[
x+Q_1y+Q_2z
\le a+aQ_1+(a-1)Q_2
=Q_3-\bigl(b(a+2)-a\bigr)<Q_3.
\]
Hence $z=a$, after which $x+(a+1)y=b(a+1)$ forces $(x,y)=(0,b)$.
Consequently every bounded solution of one equation is
\[
(x,y,z;k)\in
\{(0,0,0;0),\ \pm(b,a,-1;0),\ \pm(0,b,a;1)\}.
\]
The terminal pairs of these triples are
$(0,0)$, $\pm(a,-1)$, and $\pm(b,a)$.  If $e_4\ne0$ and the $t=2$
triple is $\pm(0,b,a)$, its initial pair $\pm(0,b)$ cannot be a terminal
pair of the $t=1$ triple.  If instead the $t=2$ triple is
$\pm(b,a,-1)$, the overlap forces the $t=1$ triple to be
$\pm(0,b,a)$, whose initial pair $\pm(0,b)$ cannot be a terminal pair of
the $t=0$ triple.  Both alternatives are impossible, and therefore
$e_4=0$.

Suppose next that $s=-1$.  Put $C=a-b$ and $d=a-1$; then
$2\le C\le d$ and
\[
(Q_1,Q_2,Q_3)=(a,a^2-b,a^3-2ab).
\]
For one equation $x+ay+Q_2z=kQ_3$, again it suffices to take $k=0,1$.
When $k=0$, the inequality $2Q_2>d(1+a)=a^2-1$ gives $|z|\le1$.
The choice $z=0$ gives $x=y=0$, whereas $z=1$ gives
\[
x-b+a(y+a)=0.
\]
Here $1\le y+a\le2a-1$ and $b-x\le b+a-1\le2a-3$, so necessarily
$y+a=1$ and $x=b-a=-C$; the case $z=-1$ is the negative solution.
For $k=1$, if $z\le a-2$, then
\[
x+ay+Q_2z
\le d(1+a)+(a-2)Q_2
=Q_3-\bigl(a(a-b)-2b+1\bigr)<Q_3,
\]
because $a-b\ge2$.  Thus $z=d$, and
\[
x+ay=a(a-b)-b=aC-b.
\]
Reduction modulo $a$, together with $|x|\le a-1$, leaves precisely
$(x,y)=(-b,C)$ and $(x,y)=(C,C-1)$.  Hence the complete list is
\[
(x,y,z;k)\in
\{(0,0,0;0),\ \pm(C,d,-1;0),\
  \ \pm(-b,C,d;1),\ \pm(C,C-1,d;1)\}.
\]
Its terminal pairs are $(0,0)$, $\pm(d,-1)$, $\pm(C,d)$, and
$\pm(C-1,d)$.  Since $2\le C\le d$, neither $\pm(-b,C)$ nor
$\pm(C,C-1)$ occurs in this list of terminal pairs.  Thus a $t=2$ triple
of either of the last two types cannot overlap a $t=1$ triple.  The only
remaining nonzero possibility is $\pm(C,d,-1)$; it forces the $t=1$
triple to be $\pm(-b,C,d)$, whose initial pair $\pm(-b,C)$ cannot overlap
a $t=0$ triple.  Therefore $e_4=0$ also in this chamber.

In either sign, every nonzero triple in the corresponding list has nonzero
last coordinate.  It follows successively from the equations at
$t=2,1,0$ that $e_2=e_3=e_4=0$, then $e_1=0$, and finally $e_0=0$.
Thus the kernel is trivial also at $m=3$.  The finite computation described in
the data-availability statement includes this case as regression testing; it
is not used as proof of the parameter-uniform statement.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Sharp threshold and extremal bases}

\begin{theorem}[Exact quadratic-Pisot threshold]
\label{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}
Let $\beta$ be an arbitrary quadratic Pisot number and use exactly the
greedy language, Parry data, rank, cyclic modulus and fold of
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}.  Then
\[
m_*(\beta)=
\begin{cases}
3,&\beta\text{ is the root of }x^2-ax-a,\quad a\ge1,\\
3,&\beta\text{ is the root of }x^2-ax+1,\quad a\ge3,\\
2,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
More strongly, for every $m\ge m_*(\beta)$ and every $n\ge m$, the
finite-block sliding fold is injective and
\begin{equation}
|\mathcal L_n(Y_{\beta,m})|
=(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{n+m-1}.
\label{eq:quadratic-pisot-block-count}
\end{equation}
At these apertures $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is a topological conjugacy from the full
$(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)$-shift onto the SFT $Y_{\beta,m}$, with inverse of
memory zero and anticipation $m-1$.

Consequently the maximum of the threshold function on the full quadratic
Pisot family is three.  Its extremizers are exactly the negative-conjugate
diagonal $P^-_{a,a}$ and the positive-conjugate norm-one family
$P^+_{a,1}$.  The golden mean is the least extremizer, but it is not the
unique extremizer after passage from metallic units to all quadratic Pisot
bases.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Equality of two folded output blocks is, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:parry-rank-bijection}, equivalent to the sliding
congruences \eqref{eq:sliding-kernel} for their digit differences.  For
$m\ge3$, Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences} makes every
length-$m$ finite-block map injective.  Consecutive decoded blocks cover a
longer raw block, proving injectivity for every $n\ge m$ and the count
\eqref{eq:quadratic-pisot-block-count}.  The standard gluing argument used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:metallic-threshold-classification} then gives a local inverse
and an SFT description by forbidden output words of length at most $m+1$.

It remains to classify $m=2$.  Put
\[
\kappa=Q_2-dQ_1=
\begin{cases}b,&P^-_{a,b},\\ a-b,&P^+_{a,b}.
\end{cases}
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:parry-rank-bijection} gives the same explicit rule in
both chambers:
\[
\Fold_{\beta,2}(u,v)=
\begin{cases}
(u,v),&v<d\text{ or }u<\kappa,\\
(u-\kappa,0),&v=d\text{ and }u\ge\kappa.
\end{cases}
\]
If $d=\kappa$, the two constant raw points
$0^{\mathbb Z}$ and $d^{\mathbb Z}$ both map to the constant zero output,
so conjugacy fails.  This occurs exactly for $P^-_{a,a}$ and $P^+_{a,1}$.

Now assume $d>\kappa$, which is equivalent to $a>b$ in the negative chamber
and to $b>1$ in the positive chamber.  We prove finite-block injectivity,
including the boundary digits, rather than inferring it from a two-sided
inverse.  Let a raw triple $(a_0,a_1,a_2)$ produce
\[
y_i=(p_i,q_i)=\Fold_{\beta,2}(a_i,a_{i+1}),\qquad i=0,1.
\]
First recover the central digit from the two output symbols:
\begin{equation}
a_1=
\begin{cases}
q_0,&q_0>0,\\
0,&q_0=0\text{ and }p_1=0,\\
d,&q_0=0\text{ and }p_1\in\{d-\kappa,d\}.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:m2-central-decoder}
\end{equation}
Indeed, $q_0=0$ implies $a_1\in\{0,d\}$.  If $a_1=0$, the next pair has
first output coordinate $p_1=0$.  If $a_1=d$, then $p_1=d$ when
$a_2<d$ and $p_1=d-\kappa$ when $a_2=d$.  The alternatives are disjoint
because $d>\kappa>0$.

Having found $a_1$, recover the left boundary digit by
\begin{equation}
a_0=
\begin{cases}
p_0,&q_0>0,\\
p_0,&q_0=0\text{ and }a_1=0,\\
p_0+\kappa,&q_0=0\text{ and }a_1=d.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:m2-left-decoder}
\end{equation}
The last line is exactly the wrapped case in the explicit fold rule.  Finally,
recover the right boundary digit by
\begin{equation}
a_2=
\begin{cases}
q_1,&q_1>0,\\
0,&q_1=0\text{ and }p_1=a_1,\\
d,&q_1=0\text{ and }p_1=a_1-\kappa.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:m2-right-decoder}
\end{equation}
When $q_1=0$, the raw digit $a_2$ is $0$ or $d$; these give the last two
lines, which are disjoint because $\kappa>0$.  Equations
\eqref{eq:m2-central-decoder}--\eqref{eq:m2-right-decoder} prove directly that
\[
\Phi_{\beta,2,2}:\mathcal A_\beta^3
\longrightarrow (X_2^{(\beta)})^2
\]
is injective.

For any $n\ge2$, every consecutive pair $(y_t,y_{t+1})$ decodes the raw
triple $(a_t,a_{t+1},a_{t+2})$.  These triples cover the entire raw block
$a_0\ldots a_{n+1}$, so $\Phi_{\beta,2,n}$ is injective and
$|\mathcal L_n(Y_{\beta,2})|=(d+1)^{n+1}$.  On trajectories, using the first
coordinate of the triple decoded from $(y_t,y_{t+1})$ gives a genuine
memory-zero, anticipation-one inverse.

For the two extremal loci, Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences} shows
that aperture three succeeds.  The chamber classification is exhaustive by
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}, completing the proof.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 199ms:
\subsection{Arithmetic identifiability and optimal local structure}

The threshold classification leaves three finer questions: whether a fixed
aperture identifies the parameter chamber, how many consecutive output
symbols are needed by a future-only inverse, and whether the finite-block and
finite-type bounds in Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} are
sharp.  We answer the first and third completely.  For the future-only
inverse question we
obtain the exact positive-chamber value and the exact negative-chamber value
at aperture three; the parameter-uniform negative-chamber value at larger
apertures remains an explicit interface.

\begin{theorem}[Aperture-two chamber duality and first separation]
\label{thm:quadratic-aperture-two-duality}
Let $q\ge3$ and $2\le\kappa\le q-1$, and let
\begin{align*}
\beta^-_{q,\kappa}&>1,
& (\beta^-_{q,\kappa})^2-(q-1)\beta^-_{q,\kappa}-\kappa&=0,\\
\beta^+_{q,\kappa}&>1,
& (\beta^+_{q,\kappa})^2-q\beta^+_{q,\kappa}+(q-\kappa)&=0.
\end{align*}
After identifying both digit alphabets with $\{0,\ldots,q-1\}$, their
aperture-two legal languages, ranks, folds, and sliding recodings coincide
pointwise.  Explicitly,
\[
Q_0=1,\qquad Q_1=q,\qquad Q_2=q(q-1)+\kappa,
\]
and
\[
X_2=\{(u,v):v<q-1\ \hbox{or}\ u<\kappa\},
\qquad
\Fold_2(u,v)=
\begin{cases}
(u,v),&v<q-1\ \hbox{or}\ u<\kappa,\\
(u-\kappa,0),&v=q-1\ \hbox{and}\ u\ge\kappa.
\end{cases}
\]
Moreover, these are all cross-chamber coincidences at aperture two: the pair
$(q,\kappa)$ is recovered from the digit alphabet and the fold, and its
equivalence class contains the displayed negative- and positive-conjugate
bases when $\kappa\ge2$.  For $\kappa=1$ only the negative-conjugate member
is a quadratic Pisot base in the parameter ranges of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}.

The ambiguity is first resolved at aperture three.  In low-to-high
orientation,
\[
X_3^{(\beta^-_{q,\kappa})}
 \setminus X_3^{(\beta^+_{q,\kappa})}
=\{(h,\kappa-1,q-1):\kappa\le h\le q-1\},
\]
the reverse difference is empty, and
\[
Q_3(\beta^-_{q,\kappa})-Q_3(\beta^+_{q,\kappa})=q-\kappa.
\]
Thus the complete aperture-two input--output map does not determine the sign
of the conjugate, whereas the aperture-three legal language does.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For the negative chamber take $(a,b)=(q-1,\kappa)$; for the positive chamber
take $(a,b)=(q,q-\kappa)$.  The stated parameter bounds are precisely those
which place both choices in Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}.  In both
cases $d=q-1$, while Proposition~\ref{prop:parry-rank-bijection} gives
\[
Q_2=(q-1)q+\kappa=q^2-(q-\kappa).
\]
The two length-two Parry conditions both say that a high digit $q-1$ must be
followed, in the low-to-high convention, by a digit smaller than $\kappa$.
The displayed fold now follows directly from cyclic reduction of
$u+qv$ modulo $Q_2$.  This proves pointwise equality of the two sliding maps.

Conversely, the alphabet gives $q$, and the least $u$ for which
$(u,q-1)$ is sent to $(0,0)$ gives $\kappa$.  The chamber parametrization in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers} then leaves exactly the two displayed
members when $\kappa\ge2$ and only the negative member when $\kappa=1$.

Finally, the two quasi-greedy boundary words are
\[
d_{\beta^-}^*(1)=((q-1)(\kappa-1))^\infty,
\qquad
d_{\beta^+}^*(1)=(q-1)(\kappa-1)^\infty.
\]
Their first two digits agree.  At length three, the negative boundary permits
a suffix $(q-1,\kappa-1,h)$ for every $h\le q-1$, whereas the positive
boundary requires $h\le\kappa-1$.  All other suffix tests agree.  Reversing
to the low-to-high convention gives exactly the stated set difference.
There are $q-\kappa$ such words.  Equivalently, the two recurrences give
\[
Q_3^-=(q-1)Q_2+\kappa q,\qquad
Q_3^+=qQ_2-(q-\kappa)q,
\]
whose difference is $q^2-Q_2=q-\kappa$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Open negative-chamber two-window interface]
\label{rem:negative-two-window-open-interface}
For $a\ge b\ge1$ and $m\ge4$, the natural candidate statement is that two
consecutive congruences
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_j\equiv
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{j+1}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m},
\qquad |e_j|\le a,
\]
force $e_0=0$ for the recurrence
$Q_{j+2}=aQ_{j+1}+bQ_j$.  Finite enumeration supports this assertion, but a
parameter-uniform proof requires an exhaustive bounded-carry invariant.  In
particular, sorting only by the two multiples of $Q_m$ and then appealing to
the signs of successive quotient coefficients does not prove that all carry
branches have been covered.  We therefore do not use or claim this candidate
statement below.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Future-only inverse bounds and the positive-chamber optimum]
\label{thm:quadratic-optimal-causal-inverse}
For $m\ge3$ define
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{\ell\ge1:\ a_t=\psi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+\ell-1})
\text{ for every }y=\Phi_{\beta,m}(a)\}.
\]
Then
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)=3\quad(\beta'>0),
\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,3)=2\quad(\beta'<0).
\]
For $\beta'<0$ and $m\ge4$, the proved bounds are
\[
2\le \ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)\le m.
\]

If, explicitly, $(P)\subset\mathbb Z[z]$ denotes the principal ideal
generated by $P$ and
\[
\mathfrak a(P,d)=\min\{\deg H:0\ne H\in(P),\ H(0)\ne0,
\ \max_j|[z^j]H|\le d\},
\]
then
\[
\mathfrak a(P^-_{a,b},a)=2,
\qquad
\mathfrak a(P^+_{a,b},a-1)=3.
\]
Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}=\mathfrak a$ in the positive chamber for every
$m\ge3$ and in the negative chamber at $m=3$; equality in the negative
chamber for $m\ge4$ is precisely the interface in
Remark~\ref{rem:negative-two-window-open-interface}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose first that $P=P^-_{a,b}$, so $d=a$.  The coefficient vector
$(b,a,-1)$ of $-P$ is the difference of two digit blocks and has nonzero
first coordinate.  Padding it by zeros gives two raw length-$m$ words with
the same single output, so one output never suffices.  Conversely, equality
of two consecutive outputs at $m=3$ gives two overlapping triples from the
complete negative-chamber list in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences}.  A triple in that list with
nonzero first coordinate is necessarily $\pm(b,a,-1)$; the next triple would
then begin with $\pm(a,-1)$, which is not the initial pair of any triple in
the list.  Hence $e_0=0$, and two outputs determine the first digit at
$m=3$.  For $m\ge4$, the lower bound remains two, while
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences} shows that $m$ consecutive
outputs determine the entire overlapping raw block and hence its first digit.

Now let $P=P^+_{a,b}$, so $d=a-1$.  The polynomial
\[
(1+z)P(z)=b+(b-a)z+(1-a)z^2+z^3
\]
has all coefficients in $[-d,d]$ and nonzero constant term.  Its positive
and negative parts, padded by zeros, give two raw blocks with different first
digits and the same two consecutive outputs.  Indeed, the first weighted sum
is zero when $m\ge4$ and is $-Q_3$ when $m=3$, while the shifted sum is zero.
Thus two outputs do not suffice.

Three outputs do suffice.  For $m=3$ this is
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences}.  For $m\ge4$, apply the
elimination leading to \eqref{eq:quadratic-elimination} to the first three
equal-output congruences.  Since here $s=-1$ and $\delta=0$, it gives
\[
e_0+bQ_{m-1}e_{m+1}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\]
If $e_{m+1}\ne0$, Lemma~\ref{lem:nearest-multiple-separation} makes the
distance of the second term from $Q_m\mathbb Z$ at least
$Q_{m-2}>a-1\ge|e_0|$, a contradiction.  Hence $e_{m+1}=0$, and then
$|e_0|<Q_m$ forces $e_0=0$.

Finally, no nonzero polynomial of degree below two belongs to $(P_\beta)$.
For $P^-_{a,b}$ the polynomial $-P^-_{a,b}$ has coefficient norm $a$, so
$\mathfrak a(P^-_{a,b},a)=2$.  For $P^+_{a,b}$ every degree-two member of
the integral ideal is a nonzero integral multiple of $P^+_{a,b}$ and has
coefficient norm at least $a>d$, while $(1+z)P^+_{a,b}$ has coefficient norm
at most $a-1$.  Hence $\mathfrak a(P^+_{a,b},a-1)=3$, proving the last
assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Critical two-fixed-point normal form]
\label{thm:quadratic-critical-normal-form}
Let $\beta$ be an extremizer in
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}, put
$q=\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1$ and $d=q-1$, and consider its critical map
$\Phi_{\beta,2}$.  Then
\[
\Fold_{\beta,2}(u,v)=
\begin{cases}
(u,v),&(u,v)\ne(d,d),\\
(0,0),&(u,v)=(d,d).
\end{cases}
\]
Its only nonsingleton two-sided fiber is
\[
\Phi_{\beta,2}^{-1}((00)^{\mathbb Z})
=\{0^{\mathbb Z},d^{\mathbb Z}\}.
\]
Consequently
\[
h_{\rm top}(Y_{\beta,2})=\log q,
\qquad
\#\operatorname{Fix}(\sigma^n|_{Y_{\beta,2}})=q^n-1,
\qquad
\zeta_{Y_{\beta,2}}(z)=\frac{1-z}{1-qz}.
\]
The critical image is strictly sofic.  Its right Fischer cover has vertices
$0,\ldots,d$ and one edge $u\to v$ for every ordered pair; the edge label is
$(u,v)$ except that $d\to d$ is labeled $(0,0)$.  The cover is bi-resolving,
and the factor is one-to-one away from the two fixed points above.  Hence, in
the standard terminology, the critical image is a near-Markov AFT shift and
its multiplicity graph is $K_{2,1}$: the two exceptional domain orbits lie
over the single exceptional image orbit.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At either extremal locus the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} has $\kappa=d$, which gives
the displayed rule.  Every nonzero output symbol records its raw edge
uniquely and hence propagates the raw digits uniquely in both directions.
If every output symbol is $(0,0)$, each raw edge is either $0\to0$ or
$d\to d$; overlap forces one of the two constant paths.  This proves the
fiber statement.

The map is finite-to-one from the full $q$-shift, so entropy is $\log q$.
A point fixed by $\sigma^n$ outside the exceptional image has a unique
preimage and that preimage is fixed by $\sigma^n$.  The $q^n$ fixed points
of the full shift therefore map to $q^n-1$ fixed image points, since exactly
the two constant preimages are identified.  The zeta identity follows from
\[
\exp\left(\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{(q^n-1)z^n}{n}\right)
=\frac{1-z}{1-qz}.
\]

The displayed graph is right- and left-resolving and follower-separated.
Every vertex is reached by a one-symbol label different from $(0,0)$, which
is intrinsically synchronizing.  Lemma~\ref{lem:fischer-cover-criterion}
therefore identifies it as the right Fischer cover.

To see that the image is not an SFT, fix $N$ and choose $L>N$.  The word
\[
(d,0)(0,0)^L(d,0)
\]
is not admissible: the first anchor forces the intervening hidden state to be
$0$, while the second requires it to be $d$.  Every subword of length at
most $N+1$ meets at most one anchor and is admissible, using the constant
$0$ or constant $d$ hidden path as appropriate.  Thus no finite forbidden
list defines the image.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact finite-block onset, Fischer cover, and Markov order]
\label{thm:quadratic-exact-local-order}
Let $m\ge m_*(\beta)$ and define
\[
\delta(\beta,m)=\min\{n\ge1:\Phi_{\beta,m,n}\text{ is injective}\}.
\]
Then
\[
\delta(\beta,m)=m.
\]
The natural overlap graph with vertex set
$\mathcal A_\beta^{m-1}$ and edge labels $\Fold_{\beta,m}$ is the right
Fischer cover of $Y_{\beta,m}$ and has exactly
$(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{m-1}$ vertices.  In the output alphabet fixed in
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}, the image is an $m$-step SFT but
not an $(m-1)$-step SFT.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The upper bound $\delta(\beta,m)\le m$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}.  For $m\ge3$ the reverse
inequality has a uniform certificate.  In the negative chamber, place the
difference vector $(b,a,-1)$ in raw positions $m-2,m-1,m$ and zeros
elsewhere.  In the positive chamber place
$(b,b-a,1-a,1)$ in positions $m-3,\ldots,m$.  Each vector is the difference
of two digit blocks.  The recurrence shows that all $m-1$ consecutive output
ranks agree modulo $Q_m$, while the raw blocks differ.  For an invertible
aperture $m=2$, the two pairs $(0,0)$ and $(\kappa,d)$ have the same single
output, so again $\delta\ge m$.

The overlap graph is essential and irreducible because its underlying graph
is the full de Bruijn graph.  It is right-resolving: two edges with the same
initial vertex and different appended digits have rank difference
$hQ_{m-1}$ with $1\le|h|\le d$.  We verify that this difference cannot wrap
around the modulus.  In the negative-conjugate chamber,
\[
Q_m=aQ_{m-1}+bQ_{m-2}>aQ_{m-1}=dQ_{m-1}.
\]
In the positive-conjugate chamber $d=a-1$.  For $m=2$,
\[
Q_2=a^2-b>(a-1)a=dQ_1
\]
because $b\le a-2$.  For $m\ge3$, the strict ratio bound
\eqref{eq:ratio-minus} gives
\[
\frac{Q_m}{Q_{m-1}}>a-\frac{b}{a-1}>a-1=d.
\]
Thus $0<|h|Q_{m-1}<Q_m$ in either chamber, proving that the edge labels are
distinct modulo $Q_m$.  It is
left-resolving because two edges with the same terminal vertex and different
initial digits have nonzero rank difference of modulus at most $d<Q_m$.
By $\delta=m$, every output word of length $m$ labels a unique path and is
intrinsically synchronizing.  Distinct vertices have distinct follower
languages: a common right-infinite label would be decoded by
Theorem~\ref{thm:quadratic-optimal-causal-inverse} when $m\ge3$, or by
\eqref{eq:m2-central-decoder}--\eqref{eq:m2-right-decoder} when $m=2$, and
would force the two raw vertices to coincide.  The Fischer-cover criterion
now proves the cover assertion and its state count.

The gluing proof in Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} defines
$Y_{\beta,m}$ by forbidden output words of length at most $m+1$, so it is an
$m$-step SFT.  The difference certificates above give a length-$(m-1)$
output word which labels two paths ending at distinct Fischer states; for
$m=2$ the same role is played by the common label $(0,0)$.  Write the two
terminal states as $u\ne v$.  Follower-separatedness supplies, after
interchanging $u$ and $v$ if necessary, a successor word
$z\in\mathcal F^+(u)\setminus\mathcal F^+(v)$.  Choose a unique-path
intrinsically synchronizing word ending at the initial state of the path
whose common label terminates at $v$; such a word was constructed in the
Fischer-cover argument above.  Appending the common label to that prefix has
a unique continuation path by right-resolvingness, and that path ends at
$v$.  It therefore cannot be spliced to $z$, although the common label can
be followed by $z$ along the path ending at $u$.  This gives admissible left
and right extensions whose concatenation is not admissible, so the common
word is not intrinsically synchronizing.  Every word of length $r$ in an
$r$-step SFT is intrinsically synchronizing.  Hence $Y_{\beta,m}$ is not an
$(m-1)$-step SFT.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Worked cases in both chambers}

The following computations use the low-to-high convention $(u,v)$ and show
the Parry boundary, the legal rank interval, the cyclic reduction, and the
threshold classification in four genuinely nonmetallic cases.

\begin{example}[Negative conjugate, extremal]
Let $\beta$ be the positive root of $x^2-2x-2$.  Here
$(a,b,d)=(2,2,2)$,
\[
d_\beta^*(1)=(21)^\infty,\qquad (Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,3,8),
\qquad \kappa=b=2=d.
\]
At length two, legality is $v=2\Rightarrow u<2$.  Thus the legal pairs are
\[
(0,0),(1,0),(2,0),(0,1),(1,1),(2,1),(0,2),(1,2),
\]
and their ranks $u+3v$ are exactly $0,\ldots,7$.  The raw pair $(2,2)$ has
rank $8$, so cyclic reduction modulo $Q_2=8$ gives
$\Fold_{\beta,2}(2,2)=(0,0)$.  Consequently the constant raw points
$0^{\mathbb Z}$ and $2^{\mathbb Z}$ collide at aperture two.  Theorem
\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} gives $m_*(\beta)=3$, as predicted by
the negative extremal condition $a=b$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Negative conjugate, nonextremal]
Let $\beta$ be the positive root of $x^2-3x-2$.  Then
$(a,b,d)=(3,2,3)$,
\[
d_\beta^*(1)=(31)^\infty,\qquad (Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,4,14),
\qquad \kappa=b=2<d.
\]
The legal pairs obey $v=3\Rightarrow u<2$; explicitly,
\[
X_2^{(\beta)}=\{0,1,2,3\}\times\{0,1,2\}
\ \cup\ \{(0,3),(1,3)\},
\]
and their ranks $u+4v$ fill $0,\ldots,13$.  The only wrapped pairs are
\[
\Fold_{\beta,2}(2,3)=(0,0),\qquad
\Fold_{\beta,2}(3,3)=(1,0).
\]
For the raw triple $(3,3,0)$ the two outputs are
$y_0=(1,0)$ and $y_1=(3,0)$.  Formula
\eqref{eq:m2-central-decoder} reads $p_1=3\in\{d-\kappa,d\}=\{1,3\}$ and
recovers $a_1=3$; then \eqref{eq:m2-left-decoder} gives
$a_0=1+2=3$, and \eqref{eq:m2-right-decoder} gives $a_2=0$ because
$p_1=a_1$.  Hence the boundary triple is recovered from two outputs and
$m_*(\beta)=2$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Positive conjugate, extremal]
Let $\beta$ be the positive root of $x^2-3x+1$.  Here
$(a,b,d,c_0)=(3,1,2,1)$,
\[
d_\beta^*(1)=21^\infty,\qquad (Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,3,8),
\qquad \kappa=a-b=2=d.
\]
The boundary condition at length two is $v=2\Rightarrow u\le1$.  Hence the
legal pairs are
\[
(0,0),(1,0),(2,0),(0,1),(1,1),(2,1),(0,2),(1,2),
\]
with ranks $0,\ldots,7$, while $(2,2)$ has rank $8$ and is recoded to
$(0,0)$.  The constant raw points $0^{\mathbb Z}$ and
$2^{\mathbb Z}$ therefore collide at aperture two.  This is the positive
norm-one extremal family $b=1$, and $m_*(\beta)=3$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Positive conjugate, nonextremal]
Let $\beta$ be the positive root of $x^2-4x+2$.  Then
$(a,b,d,c_0)=(4,2,3,1)$,
\[
d_\beta^*(1)=31^\infty,\qquad (Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,4,14),
\qquad \kappa=a-b=2<d.
\]
The length-two boundary again gives $v=3\Rightarrow u\le1$, so the legal
ranks on
\[
X_2^{(\beta)}=\{0,1,2,3\}\times\{0,1,2\}
\ \cup\ \{(0,3),(1,3)\}
\]
are $0,\ldots,13$, and the wrapped pairs are
$(2,3)\mapsto(0,0)$ and $(3,3)\mapsto(1,0)$.  For the raw triple $(3,3,0)$,
the output pair $((1,0),(3,0))$ is decoded exactly as in the preceding
nonextremal example: $a_1=3$, $a_0=1+\kappa=3$, and $a_2=0$.  Thus the
positive-chamber nonextremal case has $m_*(\beta)=2$.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[What is and is not claimed]
\label{rem:quadratic-open-interface}
The theorem concerns the cyclic language-rank recoding of
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}.  Published finite-transducer
normalization results for Pisot bases
\cite{Frougny1992,FrougnySteiner2008,BertheFrougnyRigoSakarovitch2019}
establish recognizability for numerical representations on other redundant
digit sets and boundary conventions, but they do not identify those
normalizations with this cyclic rank map.
No threshold claim is made for signed digits, non-greedy expansions,
non-cyclic truncation, degree greater than two, or general Pisot substitution
codings.  These choices are genuine residual interfaces rather than
consequences of Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}.
\end{remark}


 succeeded in 187ms:
总裁定
不可达到。
以现稿的事实边界为限，目前没有一项已定位到原始出处的、同层级的命名公开问题，能够同时通过以下四个门槛：


结论落在既有标准对象上，而非仅落在本文的循环语言秩映射上；


不把有限 Parry 数据、simple-Parry 性或待判定的自动机结构预先作为输入；


参数一致证明可由现有整数递推、Toeplitz 同余与碰撞图完成；


存在不依赖“先猜定理再枚举支持”的决定性证伪检验。


现稿最强的通用工具——有限差分碰撞图——是在 dβ​(1) 已知有限、Qj​ 已可生成之后，精确计算本文特定滑动码的未来解码长度和双边单射性；它并不产生 β-数值等价、任意自动机的识别结构或未知 Parry 数据。  而且现稿明确区分秩同余与 Z[β] 中的数值正规化。
因此，最值得投入的方向不是再寻找术语上相近的公开猜想，而是证明一个显著加强现稿、但应诚实标为本文映射内部定理的固定基数结果。

一、经原始出处核对但不具备非循环攻击路线的命名问题
1. Ultimate Periodicity Problem for Linear Numeration Systems
出处。 É. Charlier、A. Massuir、M. Rigo、E. Rowland，Ultimate periodicity problem for linear numeration systems，International Journal of Algebra and Computation 32 (2022), 561–596，DOI 10.1142/S0218196722500254；预印本 arXiv:2007.08147v2，Problem 1。问题的输入是线性数系 U 及接受 repU​(X) 的任意 DFA，要求判定 X⊆N 是否最终周期。Eric Rowland
为什么现有工具不能攻击。 对 Pisot 数系，这一判定问题已可借助加法的 U-可识别性和相应一阶理论处理；开放部分恰在更一般的数系及任意输入 DFA，而不是本文某一固定 rank-fold 的纤维结构。Eric Rowland 现有碰撞图的状态是长度 m−1 的数字差分，边条件只有一个固定 Toeplitz 同余。它没有承载任意 DFA 的转移幺半群，也没有把“最终周期”转换成“本文滑动码单射”的构造。
真正需要的桥接引理将是：

对任意输入 (U,A)，有效构造一个 Parry 基数与窗口，使 A 所识别集合最终周期，当且仅当相应循环秩滑动码满足某一碰撞图性质。

这不是技术性补缝，而是一个能够把任意正则语言的算术语义编码进单一递推 Toeplitz 核的归约。目前工具完全没有这一表达能力。
决定性证伪。 固定同一个 U，取两个结构不同的 DFA，分别识别最终周期与非最终周期集合。任何只依赖 Qj​、m 和数字差分字母表而不实质编码 DFA 转移结构的候选归约都会对二者生成同一碰撞系统，因而立即失败。

2. Bassino 的高次数 simple β-number 分类问题
出处。 F. Bassino，Beta-expansions for cubic Pisot numbers，LATIN 2002，LNCS 2286，141–152，DOI 10.1007/3-540-45995-2_17。Bassino 在 Proposition 2 之后明确指出：对更高次数的 simple β-numbers，给出类似三次情形的系数分类仍是开放问题；定位为原文第 12 页。SciSpace 现稿参考文献亦给出了相同书目信息。
为什么现有工具不能攻击。 该问题要求从最小多项式或代数共轭数据判断 dβ​(1) 是否有限，并在有限时确定其形状。本文的 Qj​ 递推、秩区间双射和碰撞图却以有限字 t1​⋯tp​=dβ​(1) 为起点。因而直接套用这些工具等于把“β 是否 simple-Parry”作为先验输入。
非循环的最小桥接引理必须是以下二者之一：
由最小多项式有效给出一个终止界 B(P),
使得 greedy orbit 在 B(P) 步内未终止便永不终止；或者构造一个不预设周期数据的有限状态轨道判据。现有最近倍数分离和滑动核消失均不控制 Tβn​(1)，也不提供这样的终止界。
决定性证伪。 对一个未预先给出 dβ​(1) 的四次候选多项式运行拟议方法。若在建立 Qj​、模数或状态图之前仍必须输入一个有限 Parry 字，路线即属循环，直接否决。

3. 为什么一般的 sliding-block decoder 文献也不提供可接管的公开问题
有限状态编码的 decoder-window 上界并非空白领域。Ashley 已证明一般受限系统编码存在与图状态数线性相关的 sliding-block decoder window 上界，并给出相应一般下界。IEEE Xplore 因而本文能够形成的新贡献只能是某个算术生成的码族中精确的 right-closing delay／inverse anticipation，而不是一般 decoder 的存在性或一般有限性。

二、真正可达的内部定理候选
排名 1：单一固定二进制三次 Pisot 基数中的精确无界解码深度
成功概率：0.82。
预期层级：TIER‑2 下沿；若作为孤立短文则更接近 TIER‑3 上沿。
综合排名：1。
这是当前工具最有希望产生的最强新定理。它严格加强现稿的三次无界性：现稿需要让基数 βn​、数字字母表大小和窗口同时变化。 下面的目标固定一个基数、固定二进制字母表，只让窗口变化。
可投稿的标准术语定理陈述

定理（固定二进制三次基数的精确 right-closing delay）。
设 θ 为
x3−2x2+x−1
的唯一实根。则 θ∈(1,2) 是三次 simple-Parry Pisot 数，并且
dθ​(1)=11010∞.
令 Qm​ 为 greedy θ-shift 的长度 m 语言复杂度，并令
Φθ,m​ 为由长度 m 合法词的 colexicographic rank 模 Qm​
所定义的滑动块码。则：
Q0​=1,Q1​=2,Q2​=4,Q3​=7,Qr+4​=Qr+3​+Qr+2​+Qr​.
此外：
Φθ,2​=id,
Φθ,3​ 非单射，且其唯一非平凡双边纤维为
{0Z,1Z};
对每个 m≥4，Φθ,m​ 是从二进制全移位到其有限型像的拓扑共轭，并且其最优未来解码窗口恰为
ℓcau​(θ,m)=2⌊2m​⌋−1.​
等价地，逆码具有零记忆和精确预见
2⌊2m​⌋−2.
特别地，一个固定的二进制三次 simple-Parry Pisot 基数已经具有无界的最优未来解码深度。

现有工具能够直接承担的部分
θ 的 Pisot 性和 dθ​(1)=11010∞ 可直接验证：多项式仅有一个实根，另外两个根为共轭复根且模为 θ−1/2<1；关系
1=θ−1+θ−2+θ−4
及 Parry 后缀条件给出 greedy 展开。由此得到上述 Qr​ 递推。
随后，现稿 Theorem 6.2 已经把最优未来解码长度精确化为坏初态在有限差分碰撞图中的最长生存长度，且把非单射等价化为可达有向环。 因而无需建立任何新的符号动力学框架；新工作完全归结为一个固定四阶递推的参数一致有界 carry 分类。
仍缺的最小桥接引理
所需的核心不是重新假设定理，而是以下独立整数命题。

四步 carry 分类引理。
设
St​=j=0∑m−1​Qj​et+j​,ej​∈{−1,0,1}.
对四个平移行作递推消元
St​−St+1​−St+2​−St+4​.
则所有内部系数消失；在 m 充分大时，尾端三个数字及相应整数 carry 只能落入一个与 m 无关的有限状态集。该 carry 图是无环的，其非零终端路径按 m 的奇偶性具有唯一的最长类型，长度分别为 m−1 与 m−2。

它不假设 ℓcau​ 的值，也不假设单射；它分类的是消元后所有可能的整数 carry 状态。证明完成后，目标公式只是碰撞图定理的一行推论。
最短证明路线
第一步验证 Parry 数据和四阶 Q-递推。第二步建立尾端最近倍数分离：对有限个
uQm−3​+vQm−2​+wQm−1​,u,v,w∈{−1,0,1},
给出距 Qm​Z 的统一下界。第三步由四行消元得到固定 carry 状态图。第四步按奇偶性归纳分类其全部非零终端路径，并写出达到最长路径的显式差分词。第五步调用现稿的精确碰撞判据。
最便宜且有决定性的证伪测试
先不证明任何全参数命题，只对消元所得的候选 carry 状态作符号穷尽：状态包含三个边界数字和整数 carry；使用递推比值的向外舍入区间验证每一条可能转移。若出现拟议有限状态集之外的一个合法状态，或出现非零有向环，则整条路线立即否决。
作为有限预检，本轮独立枚举了完整碰撞图。得到：
mℓcau​​43​53​65​75​87​97​109​119​1211​1311​1413​1513​1615​​
并在 m=3 找到常差分环 et​≡1，即
1+2+4=Q3​=7.
这些有限结果与目标公式完全一致，但不构成参数一致证明。
影响判断
该结论仍然属于本文自定义 rank-fold；它不解决 β-正规化、Parry 数识别或一般 decoder-window 问题。然而，与现稿“随 n 改变基数和字母表”的无界族相比，固定 θ 与固定二进制字母表消除了最明显的外生参数增长解释。因此它是实质性的结构加强，而非单纯增加一个例子。

排名 2：全部二进制三次 Pisot 基数的 aperture–delay 完全分类
成功概率：0.48。
预期层级：完整实现时为 TIER‑2；仅给若干基数则降至 TIER‑3。
综合排名：2。
可投稿的标准术语定理陈述

定理（二进制三次 Pisot rank codes 的完全分类）。
设 β∈(1,2) 为三次 Pisot 数。则其最小多项式属于有限集合
​x3−2x2+x−1,x3−x2−x−1,x3−x2−1,x3−x−1,​
相应的有限 greedy 展开分别为
1101,111,101,10001.
对每个这样的 β，存在可显式给出的有限异常集
Eβ​⊂N、整数周期 hβ​ 以及有限表
Iβ​:Z/hβ​Z→{0,1},cβ​:Z/hβ​Z→Z,
使得对所有充分大的 m：
Φβ,m​ 单射⟺Iβ​(mmodhβ​)=1,
且在单射情形
ℓcau​(β,m)=m−cβ​(mmodhβ​).
所有异常 aperture、非单射周期碰撞及最优未来解码长度均由定理中的有限表完全列出。

这里“最终周期”作用于 aperture 参数 m，不是整数集合的 ultimate-periodicity problem；二者不得混同。
现有工具能够直接承担的部分
四个最小多项式的有限性可由根的系数界直接得到：若根为
β,α,αˉ，则 1<β<2、∣α∣<1，从而迹、二阶对称和与范数只能取有限多个整数值；逐个根判定后只剩上述四项。四个 finite Parry 字均可用对应多项式恒等式及后缀条件直接验证。Bassino 的三次展开分类可作为已发表的交叉核对来源，而不必重新建立整个三次分类。SciSpace
每个基数的 Qm​ 都满足固定线性递推；现稿的 Toeplitz 系统和碰撞图随后原样适用。
仍缺的最小桥接引理
需要证明一个固定递推的 carry 稳定化定理：

对上述四个 Q-递推，存在一个与 aperture m 无关的有限 carry 图，并允许转移规则依赖 mmodhβ​；所有 sufficiently large m 的差分碰撞图长路径均由该固定图控制。

这不是把完全分类重新写成假设。它把指数级、随 m 变化的 fiber-product 图压缩成固定有限图；一旦建立，最终周期性和精确延迟表由有限图计算得出。
最短证明路线
先完成排名 1 的 1101 基数。随后分别处理：


111：递推最简单，有限枚举显示 m≥4 时延迟恒为 2；


101：三步消元应给出按 mmod3 的终端路径分类；


10001：Parry 多项式分解
x5−x4−1=(x2−x+1)(x3−x−1)
暗示必须保留模 6 的边界相位；这是整个分类中唯一真正困难的部分。


最便宜且有决定性的证伪测试
对四个基数分别构造候选固定 carry 图，而不是继续扩大原始的 3m−1 状态图。若 carry 的绝对值不能获得与 m 无关的上界，或新的状态持续随 m 出现，则“最终有限表”路线应立即放弃。
本轮独立有限检查已经给出一个重要警告：对 x3−x−1 的根，aperture m=6,…,11 单射，但 m=12,13,14 又重新非单射，m=15,16 再次单射。因此任何“达到某个阈值后永久单射”的候选都已被否决；只有保留 aperture 相位的最终周期分类仍与数据相容。

三、综合排序依据
排名 1 的优势在于：桥接引理只有一个固定递推、一个二进制差分字母表和奇偶两类终端状态；有限检验已显示极整齐的精确公式。其成功后还会把现稿的“固定代数次数但变化基数”无界性提升为“固定基数、固定字母表”无界性，概念增量最大且证明风险可控。
排名 2 的潜在影响更广，但其成败取决于 10001 基数的非单调碰撞结构能否压缩为固定相位 carry 图。当前数据支持“复杂但有限”，尚不足以支持最终周期性证明。因此其期望值低于排名 1。
第三项不列入。 向一般 simple-Parry 基数、任意 Parry 基数或任意正则数系扩张时，Parry 多项式的互补因子可具有非 cyclotomic 的复根，当前工具尚不能保证 aperture 参数上的 carry 状态稳定；此时既没有参数一致桥接，也没有比原碰撞图指数界更强的可证目标。列入第三项会违反用户规定的“标准对象桥接、非循环性、参数一致性及自身证伪测试”四项合取。

核验边界
我已亲自核验：


现稿关于 rank congruence 非 β-value normalization 的范围声明；


Theorem 6.2 的 Toeplitz／碰撞图精确公式；


Bassino 开放问题和 Charlier–Massuir–Rigo–Rowland Problem 1 的原始定位；


θ 的多项式恒等式、Parry 字 1101、Qm​ 递推；


排名 1 在 2≤m≤16 的完整有限碰撞图；


四个二进制三次 Pisot 多项式的系数界枚举及上述有限 aperture 数据。


尚未核验为定理的是：


排名 1 的参数一致四步 carry 完备分类；


排名 2 的固定 carry 图存在性及最终周期表；


“未发现其他可达命名公开问题”依赖本轮检索所覆盖的文献数据库，不能解释为对全部未数字化文献的逻辑穷尽。


现稿自身亦明确规定计算脚本只作回归检验而非参数一致证明；上述有限新检验同样仅承担证伪筛查，不承担证明。


 succeeded in 206ms:
We next show that, even in algebraic degree three, the future-only inverse lengths at
injective apertures have no uniform bound.  Bassino's classification
\cite{Bassino2002} contains the cubic Pisot family and its finite expansion
of one used below.  We include a direct verification of those input data so
that the new part of the argument is visibly confined to the sliding
collision depth.

For $n\ge4$, put
\begin{equation}
M_n(x)=x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial}
\end{equation}
and define the word
\begin{equation}
w_n=n\,1\,2\cdots(n-2)\,n\,1\,0\,
(n-1)(n-2)\cdots2\,0\,n.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-word}
\end{equation}
Thus $|w_n|=2n+2$.

\begin{lemma}[Bassino's cubic family: direct verification of the required data]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data}
The polynomial $M_n$ has a unique real root $\beta_n$, this root belongs to
$(n,n+1)$, and $\beta_n$ is a nonintegral cubic Pisot number.  Moreover,
\[
d_{\beta_n}(1)=w_n0^\infty.
\]
If $(Q_j)$ denotes its Parry--Bertrand language counts, then, for
$0\le r\le n-1$,
\begin{align}
Q_0&=1,\qquad Q_1=n+1,\qquad Q_2=n^2+n+2,
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}\\
Q_r&=(n+2)Q_{r-1}-2nQ_{r-2}+nQ_{r-3}
\qquad(3\le r\le n-1).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}
\end{align}
In particular $Q_{j+1}>nQ_j$ throughout this range.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One has
\[
M_n(n)=-n<0,
\qquad
M_n(n+1)=n^2-n-1>0.
\]
The identity
\[
M_n(x)=x^2(x-2)-n(x-1)^2
\]
shows that $M_n(x)<0$ for $x\le n$.  Indeed this is immediate for
$x\le2$, while for $2<x\le n$ one uses
\[
\frac{x^2(x-2)}{(x-1)^2}
=x-\frac{x}{(x-1)^2}<x\le n.
\]
If all three roots were real, every root other than the one in $(n,n+1)$
would also have to exceed $n$; their sum could then not equal $n+2$.
Consequently $\beta_n$ is the unique real root.  A reducible monic cubic
over $\mathbb Z$ has an integral root, which is impossible because the only
real root lies strictly between consecutive integers.  Thus $M_n$ is
irreducible.  Its two remaining roots are conjugate, and their common
modulus is
\[
\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_n}}<1
\]
by the product of the roots.  This proves the asserted Pisot and degree
statements.

Let $P_{w_n}(x)=x^{2n+2}-\sum_{i=1}^{2n+2}(w_n)_i
x^{2n+2-i}$ be the Parry polynomial of the displayed word.  Direct
multiplication gives the polynomial identity
\begin{equation}
P_{w_n}(x)
=M_n(x)\frac{(x^n-1)(x^{n+1}-1)}{(x-1)^2}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-factorization}
\end{equation}
Hence $w_n$ represents one in base $\beta_n$.  The digit $n$ occurs only
at the first, the $n$th, and the last positions.  The suffix beginning at
the $n$th position starts with $n10$, whereas $w_n$ starts with $n12$;
the last suffix is $n0^\infty$.  Every other proper suffix starts with a
digit below $n$.  All proper suffixes are therefore lexicographically
smaller than $w_n0^\infty$, so Parry's criterion gives the claimed greedy
expansion.

For $r\le n-1$, its first $r$ digits are $t_1=n$ and $t_i=i-1$ for
$2\le i\le r$.  Formula~\eqref{eq:simple-parry-Q} becomes
\begin{equation}
Q_r=1+nQ_{r-1}+\sum_{i=2}^r(i-1)Q_{r-i}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q}
\end{equation}
This gives \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}, proves $Q_r>nQ_{r-1}$,
and, by subtracting the formula at the preceding three indices, gives
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}.
\end{proof}

For the next lemma write $S_k=\sum_{j=0}^kQ_j$ and
$D_k=Q_k-nQ_{k-1}$.  Formula
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q} and the cubic recurrence give,
respectively,
\begin{align}
D_k&=1+\sum_{i=2}^k(i-1)Q_{k-i},
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-positive}\\
D_k&=2Q_{k-2}-(n-2)Q_{k-3}+2S_{k-4}
\qquad(k\ge3),
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate}
\end{align}
where $S_{-1}=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Parameter-uniform two-window carry exclusion]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window}
Let $n\ge4$ and $2\le r\le n-1$.  If
$e_0,\ldots,e_r\in[-n,n]\cap\mathbb Z$ and
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}Q_je_j=c_0Q_r,
\qquad
\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}Q_je_{j+1}=c_1Q_r
\]
for integers $c_0,c_1$, then $c_0,c_1\in\{-1,0,1\}$ and $c_1=0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $Q_{j+1}>nQ_j$, geometric summation gives
\begin{equation}
S_k<\frac{n}{n-1}Q_k.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound}
\end{equation}
It follows that $nS_{r-1}<2Q_r$, so every carry is in
$\{-1,0,1\}$.  We also record the possible last digit of one window.  If
the carry is zero, \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound} gives
$nS_{r-2}<2Q_{r-1}$, so the last digit belongs to $\{-1,0,1\}$.
For a carry equal to one, the last digit is $n$.  To see this, note first
that, for $r\ge3$,
\[
D_r+D_{r-1}>Q_{r-2}+3Q_{r-3}>(n+3)Q_{r-3}
>nS_{r-3}.
\]
Together with $Q_{r-1}=nQ_{r-2}+D_{r-1}$, this says
\begin{equation}
Q_r-(n-1)Q_{r-1}>nS_{r-2}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-last-digit-gap}
\end{equation}
The case $r=2$ follows directly from
$Q_2-(n-1)Q_1=n+3>n$.  Thus a last digit at most $n-1$ cannot produce
the sum $Q_r$.  Sign symmetry gives last digit $-n$ for carry $-1$.

Suppose now, toward a contradiction, that $c_1=1$; the negative case is
obtained by changing all signs.  Put $f_j=e_{j+1}$ for
$0\le j\le r-2$.  The last-digit conclusion gives $e_r=n$, and hence
\begin{equation}
A:=\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_jf_j=D_r.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-prefix-D}
\end{equation}
Set $B=\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_{j+1}f_j$.  We shall prove
\begin{equation}
n<B<Q_r-n.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap}
\end{equation}
The two small apertures are direct.  If $r=2$, then $f_0=2$ and
\[
B=2(n+1),\qquad B-n=n+2>0,qquad
Q_2-B-n=n(n-2)>0.
\]
If $r=3$, equation
$f_0+(n+1)f_1=n+4$ and the digit bounds give
\[
(f_0,f_1)=(3,1)\quad\hbox{or}\quad(2-n,2).
\]
The corresponding values are
\[
B_1=n^2+4n+5,
\qquad
B_2=n^2+3n+6.
\]
Both exceed $n$, while
\[
Q_3-B_1-n=n^3-2n-1>0,
\qquad
Q_3-B_2-n=n^3-n-2>0.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap} holds at $r=2,3$.
The uniform argument starts at $r=4$; in particular, there is no
uncovered small aperture.

Assume $r\ge4$.  Let $\alpha,\overline\alpha$ be the nonreal conjugates
of $\beta=\beta_n$, and put
\[
s=\alpha+\overline\alpha=n+2-\beta,
\qquad p=\alpha\overline\alpha=\frac n\beta.
\]
Since $n<\beta<n+1$,
\begin{equation}
0<\frac{n}{n+1}<p<1.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-p-range}
\end{equation}
For $u_j=Q_{j+1}-\beta Q_j$, the cubic recurrence with its dominant
$\beta$ component removed gives
\[
u_{j+2}=su_{j+1}-pu_j.
\]
Set $c=p-s^2/4$.  The conjugates are nonreal, so $c>0$.  Define
\[
I_j=u_{j+1}^2-su_ju_{j+1}+pu_j^2.
\]
Direct substitution of the recurrence gives
\begin{align*}
I_{j+1}
&=(su_{j+1}-pu_j)^2
  -su_{j+1}(su_{j+1}-pu_j)+pu_{j+1}^2\\
&=p\bigl(u_{j+1}^2-su_ju_{j+1}+pu_j^2\bigr)
=pI_j.
\end{align*}
Thus $I_j=p^jI_0$.  Its two exact completed-square forms are
\begin{align*}
I_j&=\left(u_{j+1}-\frac{s}{2}u_j\right)^2
 +cu_j^2,\\
I_j&=p\left(u_j-\frac{s}{2p}u_{j+1}\right)^2
 +\frac{c}{p}u_{j+1}^2.
\end{align*}
Finally, direct substitution of $M_n(\beta)=0$ gives
\[
4c-I_0
=\frac{(\beta-n)(2\beta+n-4)}{\beta}>0.
\]
It follows from the first completed square that
\[
u_j^2\le\frac{I_j}{c}=p^j\frac{I_0}{c}<4p^j\le4,
\]
and from the second that
\[
u_{j+1}^2\le\frac{pI_j}{c}
=p^{j+1}\frac{I_0}{c}<4p^{j+1}<4.
\]
Thus $|u_j|<2$ throughout the required range.  Since
$|f_j|\le n$, this yields
\begin{equation}
|B-\beta D_r|
=\left|\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}u_jf_j\right|<2n(r-1).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-conjugate-error}
\end{equation}

The positive formula for $D_r$ gives $D_r>Q_{r-2}\ge Q_2$.  Since
$r\le n-1$ and $\beta>n$, the lower margin is explicitly
\begin{align*}
B-n
&>nQ_2-2n(r-1)-n\\
&\ge nQ_2-2n(n-2)-n\\
&=n^3-n^2+5n
=n(n^2-n+5)>0.
\end{align*}
For the upper bound, \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-geometric-bound} and
$Q_{r-3}>nQ_{r-4}$ give
\[
2S_{r-4}<\frac{2}{n-1}Q_{r-3}
\le(n-2)Q_{r-3}.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate} yields
$D_r<2Q_{r-2}<Q_{r-1}/2$.  Using $\beta-1<n$,
\[
Q_r-\beta D_r
=nQ_{r-1}-(\beta-1)D_r
>\frac n2Q_{r-1}.
\]
As $Q_{r-1}\ge Q_3=n^3+n^2+3n+4$, the upper margin is therefore
\begin{align*}
Q_r-B-n
&>\frac n2Q_{r-1}-2n(r-1)-n\\
&\ge\frac n2Q_3-2n(n-2)-n\\
&=\frac n2\bigl(n^3+n^2-n+10\bigr)>0.
\end{align*}
The last polynomial is positive for $n\ge4$: its derivative
$3n^2+2n-1$ is positive there, and its value at $4$ is positive.
This proves \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap} uniformly for
$4\le r\le n-1$.

The first window sum is $e_0+B$.  By
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-shifted-gap} and $|e_0|\le n$, it lies strictly
between $0$ and $Q_r$, contradicting its membership in $Q_r\mathbb Z$.
Therefore $c_1=0$.
\end{proof}

For $L\ge1$, let $\mathcal K_{n,r,L}$ be the set obtained from
$\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,L}$ by allowing the first coordinate to vanish.
Thus it consists of all $e\in[-n,n]^{r+L-1}\cap\mathbb Z^{r+L-1}$ whose
$L$ sliding sums vanish modulo $Q_r$.  Define
\[
E_{n,2}=(2,n),
\qquad
E_{n,3}=(n-2,-2,-n,1),
\qquad
E_{n,r+1}=(-2,E_{n,r},0)\quad(r\ge3).
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact terminal collision paths]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-terminal-paths}
For every $n\ge4$ and $2\le r\le n-1$,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal K_{n,r,r-1}=\{0,\ E_{n,r},\ -E_{n,r}\},
\qquad
\mathcal K_{n,r,r}=\{0\}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-terminal-classification}
\end{equation}
For $r\ge3$, the exact window sums of $E_{n,r}$ are
$-Q_r,0,\ldots,0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The identity \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate} is equivalently
\begin{equation}
-2\sum_{j=0}^{k-4}Q_j+(n-2)Q_{k-3}-2Q_{k-2}
-nQ_{k-1}+Q_k=0
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-zero-word}
\end{equation}
for $k\ge3$; for $k=2$ the corresponding identity is
$-2-nQ_1+Q_2=0$.  Sliding these identities through the displayed vector
$E_{n,r}$ proves directly that its window sums are $-Q_r,0,\ldots,0$.
The vector $E_{n,2}$ has its sole sum equal to $Q_2$.

We prove the classification by induction on $r$.  At $r=2$, the bound
$|e_0+(n+1)e_1|<2Q_2$ shows
\[
\mathcal K_{n,2,1}=\{(0,0),\ \pm(2,n)\}.
\]
For two windows, Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window} makes the
second carry zero.  Then $e_1+(n+1)e_2=0$ forces $e_1=e_2=0$, and the
first congruence forces $e_0=0$.  Hence
$\mathcal K_{n,2,2}=\{0\}$.

The case $r=3$ can also be read without any omitted branch.  In a
two-window path the second carry is zero, so
\[
e_1+(n+1)e_2+Q_2e_3=0.
\]
The last-digit bound in the preceding lemma gives
$e_3\in\{-1,0,1\}$.  If $e_3=0$, division by $Q_1=n+1>n$ gives
$e_1=e_2=0$; if $e_3=1$, it gives $(e_1,e_2)=(-2,-n)$; the remaining
case is its negative.  The first congruence then determines
$e_0=n-2$, $0$, or $2-n$, respectively.  Thus
$\mathcal K_{n,3,2}=\{0,\pm E_{n,3}\}$.  For three windows, apply the
already proved $r=2$ two-window classification to the middle vector
$(e_1,e_2,e_3)$.  It gives $(e_1,e_2,e_3)=0$; the last original window
then gives $Q_2e_4=0$, and the first gives $e_0=0$.  Hence
$\mathcal K_{n,3,3}=\{0\}$.

Now let $r\ge4$ and assume both assertions at aperture $r-1$.  Take
$e=(e_0,\ldots,e_{2r-3})\in\mathcal K_{n,r,r-1}$, and write
\begin{equation}
W_t:=\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}Q_je_{t+j}=c_tQ_r
\qquad(0\le t\le r-2).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-induction-windows}
\end{equation}
Repeated application of Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window} gives
$c_1=\cdots=c_{r-2}=0$.  Remove $e_0$ and $e_{2r-3}$ and denote the
middle vector by
\[
x=(e_1,\ldots,e_{2r-4}).
\]
It has length $2r-4$, the length required for $r-2$ windows of aperture
$r-1$.  For $0\le t\le r-3$, its $t$th reduced window is the original
zero-carry window $W_{t+1}$ with its last term removed:
\begin{equation}
V_t:=\sum_{j=0}^{r-2}Q_je_{t+1+j}
=-Q_{r-1}e_{t+r}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-reduced-window}
\end{equation}
Every $V_t$ is therefore a multiple of $Q_{r-1}$, so induction gives
\[
x\in\mathcal K_{n,r-1,r-2}
=\{0,E_{n,r-1},-E_{n,r-1}\}.
\]
Write $z=e_{2r-3}$ for the omitted final coordinate.  The last original
zero-carry window is
\begin{equation}
V_{r-3}+Q_{r-1}z=0.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-final-coordinate}
\end{equation}
For each of the three possible middle vectors, its last reduced window
sum is zero; hence \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-final-coordinate} gives
$z=0$.

It remains to determine the initial coordinate and to exclude all other
first carries.  Put
\[
B(x):=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}Q_je_j.
\]
The already verified window sums of
$E_{n,r}=(-2,E_{n,r-1},0)$ give
\[
\begin{array}{c|ccc}
x&0&E_{n,r-1}&-E_{n,r-1}\\ \hline
B(x)&0&2-Q_r&Q_r-2.
\end{array}
\]
The first window equation is
\begin{equation}
e_0+B(x)=c_0Q_r.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-first-coordinate}
\end{equation}
Because $|e_0|\le n$ and $Q_r\ge Q_2>n+2$, it has exactly the following
solutions:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c}
x&z&c_0&e_0&e\\ \hline
0&0&0&0&0\\
E_{n,r-1}&0&-1&-2&E_{n,r}\\
-E_{n,r-1}&0&1&2&-E_{n,r}.
\end{array}
\]
No different first carry supplies another admissible initial coordinate.
This proves the first classification.

Finally take
$e=(e_0,\ldots,e_{2r-2})\in\mathcal K_{n,r,r}$ and write its carries as
$c_0,\ldots,c_{r-1}$.  Repeated use of
Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window} gives
$c_1=\cdots=c_{r-1}=0$.  Remove $e_0$ and $e_{2r-2}$.  Formula
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-reduced-window}, now for
$0\le t\le r-2$, shows explicitly that
\[
(e_1,\ldots,e_{2r-3})\in\mathcal K_{n,r-1,r-1}=\{0\}.
\]
The last original window consequently reads
$Q_{r-1}e_{2r-2}=0$, so $e_{2r-2}=0$.  The first window then reads
$e_0=c_0Q_r$.  Since $|e_0|\le n<Q_r$, it follows that
$e_0=c_0=0$.  Thus $\mathcal K_{n,r,r}=\{0\}$, with every carry and
coordinate exhausted.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Bassino family: unbounded future-only inverse length]
\label{thm:cubic-unbounded-causal-depth}
For Bassino's family, let $n\ge4$, let $\beta_n\in(n,n+1)$ be the root of
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial} and put $m_n=n-1$.  Then $\beta_n$
is a nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot number, $\Phi_{\beta_n,m_n}$ is
injective, and
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=n-1.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-causal-length}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\sup\left\{\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m):
\substack{\beta\text{ nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot},\\
m\ge2,\ \Phi_{\beta,m}\text{ injective}}\right\}=\infty.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data} supplies all base and language
hypotheses.  At $r=m_n$, Lemma
\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-terminal-paths} gives
\[
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r-1}=\{E_{n,r},-E_{n,r}\},
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r}=\varnothing.
\]
The exact collision criterion, Theorem
\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}, now gives
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=m_n=n-1$.  Its finiteness implies
injectivity by Corollary~\ref{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}, and
letting $n$ tend to infinity proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}


 succeeded in 182ms:

## Closest beta-expansion and normalization literature

1. W. Parry, *On the beta-expansions of real numbers*, Acta Math. Acad. Sci.
   Hungar. **11** (1960), 401-416. DOI:
   `10.1007/BF02020954`. No arXiv record (publication predates arXiv).
   Used for the lexicographic admissibility criterion and `d_beta^*(1)`;
   the paper does not study cyclic finite-window folds or overlap thresholds.

2. C. Frougny, *Representations of numbers and finite automata*, Math.
   Systems Theory **25** (1992), 37-60. DOI:
   `10.1007/BF01305290`. No arXiv record located. Used only for the established
   finite-automaton normalization context; its automata are not presented as
   the cyclic rank-modulo-`Q_m` maps classified here.

3. C. Frougny and W. Steiner, *Minimal weight expansions in Pisot bases*,
   J. Math. Cryptology **2** (2008), 365-392. arXiv:`0803.2874`;
   DOI:`10.1515/JMC.2008.017`. It proves automaton recognizability for
   minimal-weight expansions in Pisot bases, not the present greedy
   Parry-rank fold or a sharp window threshold.

4. V. Berthe, C. Frougny, M. Rigo and J. Sakarovitch, *The carry propagation
   of the successor function*, Adv. Appl. Math. **120** (2020), 102062.
   arXiv:`1907.01464`; DOI:`10.1016/j.aam.2020.102062`. It studies amortized
   carry propagation in several numeration systems, including beta-numeration;
   it does not classify sliding-window conjugacy.

5. T. Hejda and W. Steiner, *Beta-expansions of rational numbers in quadratic
   Pisot bases*, Acta Arith. **183** (2018), 35-51.
   arXiv:`1411.2419`; DOI:`10.4064/aa8260-11-2017`. This is the closest
   full-quadratic-Pisot parameter comparison found. It studies purely periodic
   rational beta-expansions and computes `gamma(beta)`, not finite-window
   normalization or overlap injectivity.

6. B. Adamczewski, C. Frougny, A. Siegel and W. Steiner, *Rational numbers
   with purely periodic beta-expansion*, Bull. Lond. Math. Soc. **42** (2010),
   538-552. arXiv:`0907.0206`; DOI:`10.1112/blms/bdq019`. Relevant to
   periodic beta-expansions and Pisot-unit restrictions, but not to the cyclic
   fold threshold.

7. C. Kalle and W. Steiner, *Beta-expansions, natural extensions and multiple
   tilings associated with Pisot units*, Trans. Amer. Math. Soc. **364**
   (2012), 2281-2318. arXiv:`0907.2676`;
   DOI:`10.1090/S0002-9947-2012-05362-1`. It develops natural extensions and
   tilings arising from greedy beta-transformations; the abstract explicitly
   traces that geometric construction to Rauzy and Thurston. It does not
   contain the rank-modulo finite-window code studied here.

8. M. Minervino and W. Steiner, *Tilings for Pisot beta numeration*,
   arXiv:`1310.1277`. This treats Rauzy/beta tiles, weak finiteness, and purely
   periodic expansions for non-unit Pisot numbers; no DOI was exposed in the
   arXiv metadata checked. It supplies context for non-unit bases, not the
   claimed threshold.

## Thurston and Pisot-substitution boundary

W. Thurston's *Groups, tilings, and finite state automata* (AMS Colloquium
Lectures, 1989) is an unpublished lecture manuscript commonly cited for
beta-tiles; no arXiv ID or DOI was located.  The present manuscript therefore
does not assign it a fabricated identifier.  The traceable modern source used
for the Rauzy-Thurston statement is Kalle-Steiner, arXiv:`0907.2676`, DOI
`10.1090/S0002-9947-2012-05362-1`.

For the broader substitution interface, representative records are:

- M. Minervino and J. Thuswaldner, *The geometry of non-unit Pisot
  substitutions*, arXiv:`1402.2002`, Ann. Inst. Fourier **64** (2014),
  1373-1417, DOI:`10.5802/aif.2884`;
- V. Berthe, W. Steiner and J. Thuswaldner, *Geometry, dynamics, and
  arithmetic of S-adic shifts*, arXiv:`1410.0331`, Ann. Inst. Fourier **69**
  (2019), 1347-1409, DOI:`10.5802/aif.3273`;
- K. Nakaishi, *Pisot Substitution Conjecture and Rauzy Fractals*,
  arXiv:`2401.07771` (no DOI in the checked arXiv metadata).

These concern Rauzy fractals, substitutions, tilings, or spectral questions.
They are not predecessors of the cyclic finite-window rank fold.  Accordingly
the theorem is explicitly restricted to greedy quadratic beta-shifts and does
not claim a threshold for general Pisot substitutions.

## Novelty conclusion and limitation

The exact result not found in the checked records is the classification of the
least nontrivial aperture at which the cyclic Parry-rank fold from the full
digit shift is a conjugacy onto its SFT image, together with the two extremal
quadratic-Pisot loci.  Parry admissibility, Pisot normalization automata,
periodic expansions, beta-tiles, and Pisot-substitution geometry are cited as
prior results and are not reproved as new.  Because the Atom API itself was
rate-limited, the novelty conclusion remains a documented search conclusion,
not a claim of exhaustive bibliographic nonexistence.

## Independent refresh for the cubic upgrade and named nearest work

Checked: 2026-08-08 (Asia/Singapore).

The arXiv Atom API was queried for `"cyclic language rank"`, `"cubic
Pisot" AND "causal"`, `"Bertrand numeration" AND "sliding block"`,

Crossref confirmed the following nearest-work records and DOI metadata:

- P. B. A. Lecomte and M. Rigo, *Numeration Systems on a Regular Language*,
  Theory Comput. Systems **34** (2001), 27--44,
  DOI `10.1007/s002240010014`.  Its published abstract introduces abstract
  numeration by ordering an arbitrary infinite regular language and studies
  finite-automaton recognizability.  It supplies the ordered-language stage,
  not cyclic reduction modulo a fixed-length language count or overlap
  injectivity.
- V. Bruyere and G. Hansel, *Bertrand numeration systems and
  recognizability*, Theoret. Comput. Sci. **181** (1997), 17--43,
  DOI `10.1016/S0304-3975(96)00260-5`.  Its 26-item Crossref reference trail
  includes Bertrand-Mathis, Parry, Frougny, Cobham, Shallit, and the classical
  automata/linear-numeration literature.  It concerns Bertrand systems and
  recognizable sets, not the manuscript's rank-modulo-`Q_m` sliding map.
- F. Bassino, *Beta-Expansions for Cubic Pisot Numbers*, LATIN 2002,
  pp. 141--152, DOI `10.1007/3-540-45995-2_17`.  The abstract states that the
  expansion of one is computed for every cubic Pisot number and that cubic
  simple beta-numbers are Pisot.  Its references include Akiyama, Boyd,
  Frougny--Solomyak, Hollander, Parry, Schmidt, and Solomyak.  Consequently,
  the Pisot/simple-Parry expansion data for the candidate cubic family are
  published input, not a novelty claim.

Exact Google Scholar searches for `"cyclic language rank" Pisot` and for the
polynomial string `"x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n"` returned no matching scholarly
title.  The broader search `"cubic Pisot" "sliding block"` returned work on
beta-shifts/S-gap shifts and general automata material, not a cyclic
language-rank fold.  Crossref searches for `cubic Pisot causal inverse`,
`finite delay sliding block beta expansion`, and the exact polynomial found
Bassino and established beta-expansion/arithmetic papers but no causal-depth
or overlap-threshold predecessor.

The defensible novelty boundary is therefore narrow.  Ordered regular-
language numeration, Bertrand recognizability, cubic Pisot expansions, and
simple-Parry admissibility are prior art.  The new cubic statement is the
parameter-uniform modulus-`Q_m` collision-depth theorem.  Its required
all-parameter two-window carry exclusion and terminal-path induction are now
proved in the manuscript; no novelty is claimed for Bassino's expansion data.

The zbMATH Open API supplied the MathSciNet-style subject-index check.  Exact
title searches returned Lecomte--Rigo as Zbl `0969.68095`,
Bruyere--Hansel as Zbl `0957.11015`, and Bassino as Zbl `1152.11342`.
The Bassino query also returned Akiyama's *Cubic Pisot units with finite beta
expansions* (Zbl `1001.11038`) and the established periodic-expansion
literature.  Searches for `cubic Pisot causal inverse` and `cyclic language
rank Pisot` returned no zbMATH record.  These negative results have the same
query-language limitation as the other database searches and are not treated
as proof of bibliographic nonexistence.

## Tier-2 audit: externally posed open problems and actual interfaces

Checked: 2026-08-14 (Asia/Singapore).

### Search record and open status

The arXiv Atom API was queried for Salem-number beta expansions, purely
periodic Pisot expansions, weak finiteness, Property (F), and the Pisot
substitution conjecture.  Primary sources arXiv:1411.2419, arXiv:1912.10386,
arXiv:1810.03500, arXiv:2206.06675, and arXiv:2603.19554 were inspected in
full text.  Crossref and OpenAlex returned HTTP 429 and are recorded as
rate-limited, not as zero-result searches.  Semantic Scholar returned the
records for DOI `10.4064/aa8260-11-2017`, `10.1112/blms/12.4.269`, and
`10.1017/etds.2016.44`; its citation graph lists only two citations of
Hejda--Steiner, neither claiming a solution of the questions below.  The
zbMATH Open API was searched for `purely periodic Pisot`, `Pisot substitution
conjecture`, and `Salem Parry Schmidt`.  Its post-2018 real-base results do
not report a resolution of the Hejda--Steiner questions.  Its review of E.
Miyanohara, *A note on non-periodic greedy expansions in Salem base*, Res.
Number Theory **10** (2024), DOI `10.1007/s40993-024-00507-8`, still calls
Salem eventual periodicity an open question.  The 2022 result arXiv:2206.06675
proves that a positive-density set of powers of a Salem number are Parry,
not that the Salem number itself is Parry.  These checks support the open
status assertions below as of the audit date.

### Four printed questions

1. **Schmidt's Salem--Parry conjecture.**  K. Schmidt, *On periodic
   expansions of Pisot numbers and Salem numbers*, Bull. Lond. Math. Soc.
   **12** (1980), 269--278, DOI `10.1112/blms/12.4.269`.  Stockton's explicit
   statement of Schmidt's conjecture in *On the co-factors of degree 6 Salem
   number beta expansions*, Int. J. Number Theory **17** (2021), 1175--1200,
   DOI `10.1142/S1793042121500317`, arXiv:1912.10386, reads: "Schmidt
   conjectured that the same was true for
   Salem numbers," where "the same" is that `beta` is a Parry number, i.e.
   `d_beta(1)` is eventually periodic.  Stockton adds: "it is still not known
   for any degree greater than 4."

2. **Hejda--Steiner Question (A).**  T. Hejda and W. Steiner,
   *Beta-expansions of rational numbers in quadratic Pisot bases*, Acta Arith.
   **183** (2018), 35--51, DOI `10.4064/aa8260-11-2017`, arXiv:1411.2419,
   Section 5, final open-problems list: "Prove or disprove that
   $\gamma(\beta)=1$ for quadratic Pisot number
   $\beta>1$, a root of $\beta^2=a\beta+b$, if and only if
   $a/b\in\mathbb Z$ and $a\ge b^2$ or
   $(a,b)\in\{(24,6),(30,6)\}$."

3. **Hejda--Steiner Question (B).**  Same citation and open-problems list:
   "For which
   quadratic $\beta$ we have that $\gamma(\beta)=0$? Can we drop the
   restrictions on $a$ and $b$ in Theorem 2? More specifically, is it true
   that $a<\frac{1+\sqrt5}{2}b$ implies $\gamma(\beta)=0$?"

4. **Hejda--Steiner Question (C).**  Same citation and open-problems list:
   "What is the
   structure of the prefixes of $\beta$-adic expansions of integers for a
   general quadratic $\beta$?"

### Machinery-to-problem map

- **Schmidt.**  The finite Parry word and recurrence used in
  `thm:simple-parry-causal-collision` exist only after the desired eventual
  periodicity (and, in the paper, the stronger simple-Parry property) is
  known.  The collision graph can analyze a Salem base already known to be
  simple Parry, but it supplies no bounded orbit for `T_beta(1)`.  The missing
  step is exactly the conjecture, so applying the graph would be circular.
- **Question (A).**  `prop:parry-rank-bijection` and
  `thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold` cover precisely the roots
  `beta^2=a beta+b` and give their legal languages and count recurrence.
  However, `gamma(beta)` is defined by equality of real beta-values and the
  beta-natural-extension domain.  The paper reduces an integer language rank
  modulo `Q_m`.  A bridge would have to identify these congruence fibers with
  beta-adic/natural-extension fibers or establish the required tile-boundary
  inclusion; no such identity is present, and the paper gives an explicit
  example showing rank congruence does not preserve beta-value.
- **Question (B).**  The nearest-multiple separation lemma controls multiples
  of the integer word count `Q_m`, not the conjugate-coordinate extrema that
  determine `gamma(beta)`.  What is missing is a uniform beta-tile boundary
  estimate in the parameter range `a<phi b`.  None of the sliding congruence
  estimates has that geometric conclusion.
- **Question (C).**  The closest objects are the standard Parry language,
  its colex rank, and the prefix counts `Q_m`.  The paper's cyclic rank fold
  discards the compatible inverse-system data needed for a beta-adic integer.
  A canonical map would require compatibility of the residues as `m` varies;
  the current moduli `Q_m` do not divide one another, and no bonding maps are
  proved.  Thus the finite-window results do not determine beta-adic prefix
  structure.

### A/B/C/D assessment and selected route

- **(A):** no named problem above is honestly touched beyond sharing the
  Parry language.  The numerical-value, natural-extension, or substitution
  geometry needed by each is absent.
- **(B):** the input full shift, greedy beta-language, Parry word, language
  counts, and colex ordered-language rank are standard.  The reduction modulo
  `Q_m` is bespoke.  Above threshold the image is canonically conjugate back
  to a full shift, so it does not yield a new invariant of a standard
  beta-system.  There is no canonical map to numerical beta-normalization.
- **(C):** the Pisot hypothesis in the exact collision criterion, causal
  completeness, bounded-multiple order, and aperture-two trichotomy is an
  artifact: their proofs use only simple-Parry language/rank data and finite
  congruence graphs.  The simple-Parry hypothesis itself is real because it
  supplies the finite recurrence and positional colex rank used by the graph.
- **(D):** the negative-conjugate conjecture `ell_cau(beta,m)=2` for `m>=4`
  remains plausible, but a proof would sharpen only the bespoke decoder and
  the current carry analysis does not exhaust the nonzero constant-carry
  branches.  No plausible converse links overlap injectivity to a standard
  beta-expansion property.

Route **(C)** was selected as the best product of feasibility and impact.
Proposition `prop:nonpisot-simple-parry-scope` removes Pisot from the four
general simple-Parry results.  Strictness is proved using the irreducible
non-Pisot simple-Parry polynomial `x^4-2x^3-2`, for which
`d_beta(1)=2002 0^infinity` and `ell_cau(beta,2)=2`.  This is a genuine
hypothesis removal, but it does not solve a named problem and does not by
itself move the paper to Tier 2.
