没有发现一个可立即推翻主定理的反例或内部矛盾。


现稿尚不足以使审稿人签署“证明完整”：Lemma 5.3、Lemma 6.7、Lemma 6.8 的若干参数一致估计和穷尽步骤压缩过度。


现稿的优先权叙述存在一项确定的实质遗漏：Theorem 6.2/Corollary 6.3 所用的碰撞图—有向环—可逆性判定，属于经典的一维局部码、tessellation automata、cellular automata 和 fiber-product/pair-graph 传统，不能作为整体的新框架提出。


在修正该优先权问题并补全证明后，本文的真正新增量足以支撑 DCDS-A 论文：全部二次 Pisot 基数的精确阈值、精确有限块起点及 Markov 阶；simple-Parry 算术差分图的特殊压缩和孔径二分类；以及 Bassino 三次族上的精确无界未来解码深度。现稿不需要再附会任何具名公开问题。



一、数学正确性
1. Lemma 5.3 是当前最严重的证明审查障碍
Lemma 5.3 的最近倍数分离估计是 Lemma 5.4 乃至全部二次主定理的算术承重点。 Theorem 5.5 随后直接以 Lemma 5.4 消灭全部长度 m 的滑动差分核。
我未发现该引理的数值结论明显错误，但目前无法充分核验下列两段。
第一，正号递推情形中，式 (5.12) 的 Euclidean-division 分支在 p=r 后引入整数 w，继而从 w>0 或 w<0 直接进入两个清分母后的“穷尽终端不等式”。现稿没有完整展示这些不等式如何由前述整除条件推出，也没有在同一位置逐一处理可能的端点等号。
第二，负号递推情形从整数范数 ν 转入 δ、D、H 后，最后两个严格裕量虽然被列出，但从这些裕量到
kb−gU−​>1
的参数一致推导仍过于压缩，尤其应明确所有分母的正性以及 b=a−2、e=a−1 等边界参数。
足以消除异议的修订：


将 Lemma 5.3 分成正递推与负递推两个独立引理；


对每个 quotient/remainder 分支写出清分母前后的等价式；


列明各分母正性、严格端点以及非互素 a,b 情形；


将 w>0、w<0 两个终端分支整理成一张完整代数表，不能只陈述最终不等式；


最后单独给出从 ∣hRr​−skb∣≥1 到距离估计的闭合步骤。


**裁决影响：**若上述补充成立，本项异议消除；若不能给出，则 Theorem 5.5 及其后续精确阈值、Fischer cover 和 Markov 阶结论均失去统一证明，裁决应改为拒稿。

2. Lemma 6.7 的共轭估计需要改写为可逐行核验的独立引理
Lemma 6.7 中，前半段的 carry 范围和末位数字限制是清楚的；困难在于从三次共轭递推
uj+2​=suj+1​−puj​
转入二次型 Ij​，再由 Ij​=pjI0​ 得到 ∣uj​∣<2，最终推出
n<B<Qr​−n.
现稿把这条链压缩在极少数行内。 对上界的最后一步又仅以“减去误差后仍超过 n”结束，没有将其化为一个显式的、对 n≥4 单调为正的多项式裕量。
这里并非要求增加计算实验，而是要求证明文本本身能够表明：


0<p<1 在所用范围内为何成立；


二次型严格正定；


Ij​=pjI0​ 的直接验证；


∣uj​∣<2 所用的准确分母；


B>n 与 Qr​−B>n 的最终显式下界；


小孔径 r=2,3 与统一论证之间没有遗漏接缝。


**足以消除异议的修订：**增加一项“conjugate-error lemma”，把上述五个结论列为分项，并把最后两个裕量写成 n,r 的显式正表达式。
**裁决影响：**完成后，本项不会阻碍发表；未完成时，Theorem 6.9 的关键两窗口排除未经充分证明，三次无界定理不能接受。

3. Lemma 6.8 的归纳分类逻辑可信，但“force”步骤必须展开
Lemma 6.8 是三次主定理的另一承重点。现稿在归纳步中删除首、末坐标，将中间向量归入 Kn,r−1,r−2​，随后以“the exact window sums force the final coordinate to be zero；the first congruence then forces the initial coordinate”完成分类。 这两个“force”正是排除额外终端路径的所在，不应以文字略过。
足以消除异议的修订：


明确写出删除坐标前后各窗口编号的对应关系；


对中间向量为 0,En,r−1​,−En,r−1​ 的三个分支分别列出最后窗口和第一窗口方程；


证明所确定的首、末坐标在 [−n,n] 内唯一；


说明不存在通过改变 carry c0​ 而得到另一首坐标的可能；


最好给出一张 r↦r+1 的坐标模板，而非依赖读者自行追踪下标。


Theorem 6.9 完全依赖该终端分类把
Nβn​,r,r−1​={±En,r​},Nβn​,r,r​=∅
代入碰撞准则。
**裁决影响：**补全后可接受；若仍仅保留当前文字归纳，则三次主定理应视为证明不完整。

4. Theorem 5.9 有两个较小但确实存在的缺口
现稿为证明自然重叠图右分解，称两个同起点边的秩差 hQm−1​ 在模 Qm​ 下非零。 这不能仅由 h=0 推出，因为一般并无 gcd(Qm−1​,Qm​)=1 的统一假设。所需事实应明确写成
Qm​>dQm−1​,
或给出与此等价的距离估计。负号室中该不等式直接；正号室中需要调用已证明的比值界。
其次，从“长度 m−1 的标签词有两条路径终止于不同 Fischer 状态”到“该词不是 intrinsically synchronizing”需要一项标准引理或一个两行证明：利用 follower-separatedness 选择只属于一个终态的后继，再利用右分解性排除另一条路径的拼接。现稿直接跨过了这一逻辑。
**足以消除异议的修订：**增加上述不等式及一项“distinct terminal follower states imply non-synchronizing”引理。
**裁决影响：**这是必须修订，但不单独改变大修结论。

5. Proposition 6.5 对非 Pisot 四次例的实根计数少了一步
现稿证明了一个正根和一个位于 (−1,0) 的负根，随后直接称另外两个根为非实共轭根。 还需排除额外实根。
**足以消除异议的修订：**使用 Descartes 符号法则，或直接分析
(x4−2x3−2)′=2x2(2x−3)
的单调区间，即可完成根的计数。
**裁决影响：**纯局部缺口，修补后完全消除，不影响主裁决。

6. 其余主链条的判断
在上述问题之外，我没有发现以下结论存在明显逻辑错误：


Theorem 6.2 中有限差分图与有限窗口碰撞的对应；


Corollary 6.3 中零状态唯一前驱从而“单射 ⇒ 有限未来逆码”；


Theorem 6.4 的孔径二三分；


Theorem 5.5 的孔径二显式解码；


Theorem 5.6 的二次两室孔径二对偶；


Theorem 5.8 的唯一双点纤维和严格 sofic 性；


Theorem 5.9 的差分证书所给出的有限块起点下界；


Lemma 6.6 中三次 Pisot 性与 Parry 词的内部一致性。


机器测试的定位也是正确的：现稿明确称其为回归测试而非全参数证明。 因而测试通过不能替代上述三处解析补全，但也不存在以有限枚举冒充证明的问题。

二、新颖性与优先权
这是现稿当前最需要重写的部分。
1. ordered-language rank：基本秩结构是既有理论，不应列为本文新增量
固定语言的 lexicographic/genealogical 排序赋值、Bertrand 数系以及 Parry 语言的线性权重表示已有明确传统：Bertrand-Mathis 1989、Bruyère–Hansel 1997、Lecomte–Rigo 2000/2001，以及 Charlier–Cisternino–Stipulanti 2022 对 Bertrand-Mathis 分类的修正。后者特别指出：simple-Parry 情形具有 canonical 与 non-canonical 两套相关 Bertrand 数系。arXiv+4施普林格自然+4科学直通车+4
现稿已经引用 Bruyère–Hansel、Lecomte–Rigo 和 2022 年修正文献，但遗漏了最直接的 Bertrand-Mathis 1989 论文。更重要的是，Proposition 5.2 的“合法词按 colex 次序由
j∑​xj​Qj​
依次赋值”为 0,…,Qm​−1，以及第 6 节所称的 Parry–Bertrand rank data，本质上应列为 canonical Parry–Bertrand 数系的标准结构；反转词方向与改称 colex 不产生新的数系理论。 
本文可以保留 Proposition 5.2 的自足证明，但贡献表应改成：

“已知 canonical Parry–Bertrand positional rank 的一个针对二次两室的直接区间证明；本文的新操作始于将该固定长度秩模 Qm​ 循环化并施加滑动重叠。”

还应明确说明 simple-Parry 部分采用的是由 dβ∗​(1) 决定的 canonical 语言，而不是由有限词 dβ​(1) 产生的 non-canonical Bertrand 系统。
**裁决影响：**增加 Bertrand-Mathis 1989、解释 2022 修正并撤回“秩本身”的原创暗示后，本项问题完全可消除；不修订则优先权叙述不能接受。

2. 碰撞图、环判据和一般可逆性算法：存在确定的遗漏先行工作
现稿把 Theorem 6.2、Corollary 6.3、Theorem 6.4 一并列为“exact bounded sliding-congruence depth, causal completeness, periodic failure certificates”等新增量。 但第 6 节直到后部才用一句话称其图为标准 fiber-product collision graph 的算术特化。 这一信用不足。
一维局部映射的 injectivity/surjectivity 判定早在 Amoroso–Patt 1972 已有决策程序；Richardson 1972 处理局部变换与可逆性；Nasu 1977 明确引入左右 bundle graphs、以其刻画 injectivity 并研究逆映射；Head 1989 又直接以有标号图和有限自动机的无歧义性判定一维 cellular automaton 的 injectivity。Wolfram Media+3ACM Digital Library+3科学直通车+3
因此，下列内容不能作为整体原创主张：


以 pair/fiber graph 表示两个输入的共同输出；


“存在非对角双边路径”等价于非单射；


有限图中可达有向环给出非单射证书；


非单射可由周期碰撞检测；


单射共轭具有某个有限滑动块逆码。


有限 decoder window 及其 look-ahead 的定量问题也有独立文献传统；Ashley 的 1988、1996 工作研究有限滑动解码窗口的上界，1991 工作则属于 right-closing/resolving factor-map 理论。它们与本文的特定算术映射并不相同，但必须进入“最近框架”比较。IBM Research+2IBM Research+2
本文仍可主张的新内容是：


pair graph 在本映射中精确商化为差分状态
Δdm−1​，而不必保留输入对；


ℓcau​ 等于从首坐标非零状态出发的最长生存路径加一；


simple-Parry 权重满足零状态唯一前驱，从而一般的有限双侧逆码被加强为记忆为零的未来单侧逆码；


因差分商图而得到的具体状态数和周期上界；


孔径二的精确三分及其唯一双点纤维；


特定二次和三次族上的精确最优深度。


足以消除异议的修订：


在第 6 节开头增加“Classical pair-graph background”；


引用 Amoroso–Patt、Richardson、Nasu 1977、Head，并保留 Nasu 1995/Lind–Marcus；


把 Theorem 6.2 分为“标准 pair-graph 命题”与“本文的差分商及精确未来深度定理”；


在摘要、引言贡献表和结论中删除把图判据本身称为新框架的措辞；


将 periodic certificate 表述为“在该差分商中得到的纯周期证书及显式较小上界”，而不是首次发现周期检测。


裁决影响：这是现稿层面的致命优先权问题，但可通过完整重写而修复。完成后，我不会因此否定本文的新颖性；不完成则应拒稿。

3. resolving/right-closing 与有限延迟逆码：应作术语上的精确切割
现稿的 ℓcau​ 定义并不是泛指“某个逆滑动块码的窗口大小”，而是记忆严格为零、只读取当前及未来输出的最小长度。 这比一般共轭逆码的存在更具方向性。
建议在引言中明确写出：
ℓcau​(β,m)−1=最小的 memory-zero inverse anticipation,
并说明是否以及在何种约定下等同于 right-closing delay。若不证明这种等价，则不应把两个术语互换。
当前最稳妥的优先权划分是：


**标准部分：**共轭的逆映射仍是滑动块码；right/left resolving、right-closing、fiber product 与有限解码窗口的普通理论。


**本文部分：**本算术映射的逆码可取 memory 0，其最小 anticipation 可由差分图精确计算，并在指定族上取精确值或无界。


**裁决影响：**补充术语比较和文献后即可消除，不影响核心定理。

4. Fischer cover 与 Markov 阶：现有信用基本适当
Fischer–Krieger cover、follower separation、intrinsically synchronizing words 和最小右分解表示均属标准背景。现稿已经明确给予该理论信用。其真正的新结论可以是：


自然重叠图恰为当前输出字母表下的 right Fischer cover；


精确状态数为 (⌊β⌋+1)m−1；


有限块单射首次发生于 N=m；


在固定输出字母表中恰为 m-step 而非 (m−1)-step SFT。


现稿也正确提醒该状态数不是任意拓扑共轭下的不变量。 这组优先权主张无需实质退让，只需完成前述 Theorem 5.9 的两项局部证明。

5. 三次 Pisot 族：当前实质信用正确，但应在定理层面更醒目
Bassino 2002 已经明确给出
x3−(k+2)x2+2kx−k
这一三次 simple-β Pisot 族，并指出其 β-展开长度为 2k+2。施普林格自然+1
现稿在摘要、引言和第 6 节已经说明 cubic expansion data 来自 Bassino，而新内容是模 Qm​ 滑动碰撞深度；这一实质划分是正确的。 
仍建议将 Lemma 6.6 改名为：

Lemma 6.6 (Bassino’s cubic family; direct verification of the required data).

并在 Theorem 6.9 的陈述首句直接写“for Bassino’s family”。直接重新验证其 Pisot 性和 Parry 词有助于自足性，但不能使该族或该展开成为本文原创。
我未检出已发表工作研究这个族的“rank modulo Qm​ sliding code”的精确未来深度 n−1。因此，经过上述归属修订后，Theorem 6.9 的新颖性主张是可信的；“未检出”仍应保留为谨慎书目判断，而非书目穷尽性证明。
**裁决影响：**措辞修订后完全可接受。

6. 二次 Pisot 精确阈值：未发现直接先行结果
现有 β-shift、Parry–Bertrand 数系、Pisot 正规化 transducer、Ostrowski 约束和 resolving-code 文献分别提供语言、秩、自动机和局部码背景；但它们不等同于本文的“固定长度秩模 Qm​ 后滑动重叠”操作。现稿对此区别的说明准确。
在检索到的文献中，没有发现下列精确结论的已发表先例：
m∗​(β)=3⟺\minpoly(β)∈{x2−ax−a}∪{x2−ax+1},
以及相应的精确有限块起点和 Markov 阶。因此，在 Lemma 5.3/5.4 的统一证明获得补全后，这应当是本文最强且最稳固的原创主定理。
建议优先权措辞使用：

“To the authors’ knowledge, the exact threshold for this cyclic fixed-length language-rank recoding has not previously been determined.”

不应使用“首次解决 β-正规化阈值”等更宽泛表述。

三、DCDS-A 的发表价值与期刊适配
主题适配不存在问题。 DCDS 已发表 intermediate β-shifts 的 finite-type 分类、经典 β-shift 的 follower/predecessor/extender 集，以及 sofic shift 的规范图表示等工作。美国数学科学研究院+2美国数学科学研究院+2
在优先权收缩后，仍剩下三项足以构成 DCDS-A 论文的组合：


全部二次 Pisot 基数上的精确阈值二分及参数极值集合；


精确有限块单射起点、固定字母表 Markov 阶和 Fischer cover；


三次 simple-Parry Pisot 类中未来单侧逆码深度的显式无界族。


其中第一项提供完整分类，第三项表明二次统一现象在三次立即破裂；两者之间有清楚的动力系统叙事。故我不认为“没有解决具名公开问题”构成拒稿理由。
不过，现稿的篇章重心仍需调整。引言称 entropy、KL、pressure 等常规后果已移至补充材料， 但正文仍保留较长的 KL 缺陷、模零统计等价和 zeta 计算。  这些结果并非错误，但稀释了真正需要审查的阈值与碰撞深度证明。
建议正文集中于：


定义及 Fibonacci 原型；


二次全分类；


精确局部结构；


classical pair graph 与新差分商的明确分界；


三次无界族。


KL、Blackwell、zeta、Fourier fiber statistics 和若干算法性推论可移入补充材料。
若作者不愿进行这种动力系统导向的重构，而希望以自动机、语言秩和有限判定为中心，则 Theoretical Computer Science 是更自然的备选期刊；该刊长期发表 β-shift symbolic dynamics 和 Bertrand numeration 工作。科学直通车+1 但在完成重构的前提下，我认为无须主动降投，DCDS-A 是合理目标。

四、修订要求的分级
A. 对现稿具有致命性的事项
A1. 重写 Theorem 6.2/Corollary 6.3 的优先权定位。
必须加入 Amoroso–Patt、Richardson、Nasu 1977、Head 及 decoder-window 文献，并把标准 pair-graph 判定与新的差分商、未来深度严格分开。完成后，本项不再阻碍接受；不完成则拒稿。
A2. 补足 ordered-language rank 的直接先行文献。
必须增加 Bertrand-Mathis 1989，并解释 Charlier–Cisternino–Stipulanti 的 canonical/non-canonical 修正；Proposition 5.2 只能主张自足证明而非秩理论本身的原创。完成后可消除；不完成则优先权不可靠。
A3. 使 Lemma 5.3、6.7、6.8 达到可逐行核验程度。
这些不是可由“测试通过”替代的细节。若完整推导成立，审稿结论可转向小修或接收；若任一参数一致分支不能闭合，则相应主定理必须撤回，届时现有论文结构不足以发表。

B. 必须大修但不单独致命的事项
B1. 修补 Theorem 5.9 的右分解与非同步词论证。
B2. 补上 Proposition 6.5 的实根计数。
B3. 统一术语。
全文宜优先使用“future-only inverse length”或“memory-zero inverse anticipation”，并另行说明与 right-closing delay 的关系。
B4. 重写贡献表。
每一项应分别列出“标准输入”“本文新增算术结论”，而不能把一个标准框架及其特殊计算整体列为新增。
B5. 在 Lemma 6.6 和 Theorem 6.9 的题名或陈述中直接标注 Bassino family。
B6. 压缩非核心后果。
正文应删除或移出 KL、Blackwell、Fourier fiber、zeta 等不参与主证明的部分。
B7. 完成可复现性归档。
现稿本身承认尚无版本化公共归档和 DOI。 最终版本应提供不可变版本、校验和、运行命令及保存输出。
B8. 修订参考文献 [21]。
Drungilas–Jankauskas–Junevičius–Klebonas–Šiurys 的论文已有正式刊载版本：Bulletin of the Korean Mathematical Society 55 (2018), 1491–1501；不应只列 arXiv。韩国文化遗产委员会
完成 B1–B8 后，若 A 类事项也已解决，我的裁决将由大修改为原则上可接受、仅需小修。

C. 可选改进


合并第 2–4 节中 Fibonacci 与 metallic 子族的重复构造；


将显式 decoder 算法和较长分支表移入附录；


增加一幅图，显示普通 pair graph 到差分图
Δdm−1​ 的商化关系；


将“未发现先行工作”统一改成限定于确切映射与确切问题的审慎表述；


在摘要中把主次顺序调整为：二次全分类、三次无界深度、一般 simple-Parry 算术差分商；不要把一般图判据列在二次定理之前。


这些改进不单独影响裁决，但会显著提高论文的可信度和可读性。

最终裁决
现稿不可直接接收。建议 Major Revision，强度接近 Reject and Resubmit。
拒绝直接接收的首要理由不是结果不足，而是：


一项确定的 pair-graph/局部码先行传统尚未得到充分承认；


canonical Parry–Bertrand rank 的最直接先行工作和 2022 修正尚未准确定位；


三个核心参数一致证明仍未达到审稿人可以无保留签署的展开程度。


若作者完成上述优先权重写，并严格补全 Lemma 5.3、6.7、6.8，则我认为：


二次 Pisot 精确阈值及精确局部阶数是可信的新结果；


三次族的 ℓcau​=n−1 是可信的新结果；


合并后的增量足以发表于 DCDS-A；


届时我会支持在小修后接收。


若作者只增加若干引用而保留当前贡献表，或以有限测试代替三处统一证明，则裁决应维持拒稿。

Write a question putting THIS paper to an external expert as a REFEREE for its target journal, not as an idea generator.

Read main.tex and its sections, artifacts/ verification scripts and outputs, and artifacts/literature_check.md.

Context that must go into the question: a previous round asked this expert where the real opportunity was, and the honest answer was that no named open problem in the neighbourhood is reachable with the present machinery. So the question is no longer how to raise the tier. It is whether what we have is publishable as it stands, and what a referee at the target journal would demand.

The question must:
 - state the paper's main theorems precisely and completely, with hypotheses, in standard language;
 - state plainly what is NOT proved, including any claim that was refused or withdrawn during development;
 - separate clearly what is imported and cited from what is ours;
 - give the machine-verification evidence.

Then ask him, as a referee for that journal, to answer:
 1. Is the mathematics correct? Name any step he cannot follow or believes wrong.
 2. Is it new, and is the priority narrative accurate? Name the nearest prior work and say whether we have credited it correctly. THIS MATTERS: a referee on a sibling paper of ours just found an omitted prior result that changed that paper's whole priority claim, so treat this question as the most important of the four.
 3. Is the increment sufficient for this journal, and if not, which journal fits?
 4. What would he require in revision?

Tell him a hard rejection with reasons is more useful to us than a soft acceptance, and that every objection should come with what would resolve it.

Write next_A3_referee.txt in this paper's directory, in Chinese, formal academic register, at most 45 lines, ending with the project's standard closing line.

Report only a one-sentence summary. Do NOT git commit.

总裁定
不可达到。
以现稿的事实边界为限，目前没有一项已定位到原始出处的、同层级的命名公开问题，能够同时通过以下四个门槛：


结论落在既有标准对象上，而非仅落在本文的循环语言秩映射上；


不把有限 Parry 数据、simple-Parry 性或待判定的自动机结构预先作为输入；


参数一致证明可由现有整数递推、Toeplitz 同余与碰撞图完成；


存在不依赖“先猜定理再枚举支持”的决定性证伪检验。


现稿最强的通用工具——有限差分碰撞图——是在 dβ​(1) 已知有限、Qj​ 已可生成之后，精确计算本文特定滑动码的未来解码长度和双边单射性；它并不产生 β-数值等价、任意自动机的识别结构或未知 Parry 数据。  而且现稿明确区分秩同余与 Z[β] 中的数值正规化。
因此，最值得投入的方向不是再寻找术语上相近的公开猜想，而是证明一个显著加强现稿、但应诚实标为本文映射内部定理的固定基数结果。

一、经原始出处核对但不具备非循环攻击路线的命名问题
1. Ultimate Periodicity Problem for Linear Numeration Systems
出处。 É. Charlier、A. Massuir、M. Rigo、E. Rowland，Ultimate periodicity problem for linear numeration systems，International Journal of Algebra and Computation 32 (2022), 561–596，DOI 10.1142/S0218196722500254；预印本 arXiv:2007.08147v2，Problem 1。问题的输入是线性数系 U 及接受 repU​(X) 的任意 DFA，要求判定 X⊆N 是否最终周期。Eric Rowland
为什么现有工具不能攻击。 对 Pisot 数系，这一判定问题已可借助加法的 U-可识别性和相应一阶理论处理；开放部分恰在更一般的数系及任意输入 DFA，而不是本文某一固定 rank-fold 的纤维结构。Eric Rowland 现有碰撞图的状态是长度 m−1 的数字差分，边条件只有一个固定 Toeplitz 同余。它没有承载任意 DFA 的转移幺半群，也没有把“最终周期”转换成“本文滑动码单射”的构造。
真正需要的桥接引理将是：

对任意输入 (U,A)，有效构造一个 Parry 基数与窗口，使 A 所识别集合最终周期，当且仅当相应循环秩滑动码满足某一碰撞图性质。

这不是技术性补缝，而是一个能够把任意正则语言的算术语义编码进单一递推 Toeplitz 核的归约。目前工具完全没有这一表达能力。
决定性证伪。 固定同一个 U，取两个结构不同的 DFA，分别识别最终周期与非最终周期集合。任何只依赖 Qj​、m 和数字差分字母表而不实质编码 DFA 转移结构的候选归约都会对二者生成同一碰撞系统，因而立即失败。

2. Bassino 的高次数 simple β-number 分类问题
出处。 F. Bassino，Beta-expansions for cubic Pisot numbers，LATIN 2002，LNCS 2286，141–152，DOI 10.1007/3-540-45995-2_17。Bassino 在 Proposition 2 之后明确指出：对更高次数的 simple β-numbers，给出类似三次情形的系数分类仍是开放问题；定位为原文第 12 页。SciSpace 现稿参考文献亦给出了相同书目信息。
为什么现有工具不能攻击。 该问题要求从最小多项式或代数共轭数据判断 dβ​(1) 是否有限，并在有限时确定其形状。本文的 Qj​ 递推、秩区间双射和碰撞图却以有限字 t1​⋯tp​=dβ​(1) 为起点。因而直接套用这些工具等于把“β 是否 simple-Parry”作为先验输入。
非循环的最小桥接引理必须是以下二者之一：
由最小多项式有效给出一个终止界 B(P),
使得 greedy orbit 在 B(P) 步内未终止便永不终止；或者构造一个不预设周期数据的有限状态轨道判据。现有最近倍数分离和滑动核消失均不控制 Tβn​(1)，也不提供这样的终止界。
决定性证伪。 对一个未预先给出 dβ​(1) 的四次候选多项式运行拟议方法。若在建立 Qj​、模数或状态图之前仍必须输入一个有限 Parry 字，路线即属循环，直接否决。

3. 为什么一般的 sliding-block decoder 文献也不提供可接管的公开问题
有限状态编码的 decoder-window 上界并非空白领域。Ashley 已证明一般受限系统编码存在与图状态数线性相关的 sliding-block decoder window 上界，并给出相应一般下界。IEEE Xplore 因而本文能够形成的新贡献只能是某个算术生成的码族中精确的 right-closing delay／inverse anticipation，而不是一般 decoder 的存在性或一般有限性。

二、真正可达的内部定理候选
排名 1：单一固定二进制三次 Pisot 基数中的精确无界解码深度
成功概率：0.82。
预期层级：TIER‑2 下沿；若作为孤立短文则更接近 TIER‑3 上沿。
综合排名：1。
这是当前工具最有希望产生的最强新定理。它严格加强现稿的三次无界性：现稿需要让基数 βn​、数字字母表大小和窗口同时变化。 下面的目标固定一个基数、固定二进制字母表，只让窗口变化。
可投稿的标准术语定理陈述

定理（固定二进制三次基数的精确 right-closing delay）。
设 θ 为
x3−2x2+x−1
的唯一实根。则 θ∈(1,2) 是三次 simple-Parry Pisot 数，并且
dθ​(1)=11010∞.
令 Qm​ 为 greedy θ-shift 的长度 m 语言复杂度，并令
Φθ,m​ 为由长度 m 合法词的 colexicographic rank 模 Qm​
所定义的滑动块码。则：
Q0​=1,Q1​=2,Q2​=4,Q3​=7,Qr+4​=Qr+3​+Qr+2​+Qr​.
此外：
Φθ,2​=id,
Φθ,3​ 非单射，且其唯一非平凡双边纤维为
{0Z,1Z};
对每个 m≥4，Φθ,m​ 是从二进制全移位到其有限型像的拓扑共轭，并且其最优未来解码窗口恰为
ℓcau​(θ,m)=2⌊2m​⌋−1.​
等价地，逆码具有零记忆和精确预见
2⌊2m​⌋−2.
特别地，一个固定的二进制三次 simple-Parry Pisot 基数已经具有无界的最优未来解码深度。

现有工具能够直接承担的部分
θ 的 Pisot 性和 dθ​(1)=11010∞ 可直接验证：多项式仅有一个实根，另外两个根为共轭复根且模为 θ−1/2<1；关系
1=θ−1+θ−2+θ−4
及 Parry 后缀条件给出 greedy 展开。由此得到上述 Qr​ 递推。
随后，现稿 Theorem 6.2 已经把最优未来解码长度精确化为坏初态在有限差分碰撞图中的最长生存长度，且把非单射等价化为可达有向环。 因而无需建立任何新的符号动力学框架；新工作完全归结为一个固定四阶递推的参数一致有界 carry 分类。
仍缺的最小桥接引理
所需的核心不是重新假设定理，而是以下独立整数命题。

四步 carry 分类引理。
设
St​=j=0∑m−1​Qj​et+j​,ej​∈{−1,0,1}.
对四个平移行作递推消元
St​−St+1​−St+2​−St+4​.
则所有内部系数消失；在 m 充分大时，尾端三个数字及相应整数 carry 只能落入一个与 m 无关的有限状态集。该 carry 图是无环的，其非零终端路径按 m 的奇偶性具有唯一的最长类型，长度分别为 m−1 与 m−2。

它不假设 ℓcau​ 的值，也不假设单射；它分类的是消元后所有可能的整数 carry 状态。证明完成后，目标公式只是碰撞图定理的一行推论。
最短证明路线
第一步验证 Parry 数据和四阶 Q-递推。第二步建立尾端最近倍数分离：对有限个
uQm−3​+vQm−2​+wQm−1​,u,v,w∈{−1,0,1},
给出距 Qm​Z 的统一下界。第三步由四行消元得到固定 carry 状态图。第四步按奇偶性归纳分类其全部非零终端路径，并写出达到最长路径的显式差分词。第五步调用现稿的精确碰撞判据。
最便宜且有决定性的证伪测试
先不证明任何全参数命题，只对消元所得的候选 carry 状态作符号穷尽：状态包含三个边界数字和整数 carry；使用递推比值的向外舍入区间验证每一条可能转移。若出现拟议有限状态集之外的一个合法状态，或出现非零有向环，则整条路线立即否决。
作为有限预检，本轮独立枚举了完整碰撞图。得到：
mℓcau​​43​53​65​75​87​97​109​119​1211​1311​1413​1513​1615​​
并在 m=3 找到常差分环 et​≡1，即
1+2+4=Q3​=7.
这些有限结果与目标公式完全一致，但不构成参数一致证明。
影响判断
该结论仍然属于本文自定义 rank-fold；它不解决 β-正规化、Parry 数识别或一般 decoder-window 问题。然而，与现稿“随 n 改变基数和字母表”的无界族相比，固定 θ 与固定二进制字母表消除了最明显的外生参数增长解释。因此它是实质性的结构加强，而非单纯增加一个例子。

排名 2：全部二进制三次 Pisot 基数的 aperture–delay 完全分类
成功概率：0.48。
预期层级：完整实现时为 TIER‑2；仅给若干基数则降至 TIER‑3。
综合排名：2。
可投稿的标准术语定理陈述

定理（二进制三次 Pisot rank codes 的完全分类）。
设 β∈(1,2) 为三次 Pisot 数。则其最小多项式属于有限集合
​x3−2x2+x−1,x3−x2−x−1,x3−x2−1,x3−x−1,​
相应的有限 greedy 展开分别为
1101,111,101,10001.
对每个这样的 β，存在可显式给出的有限异常集
Eβ​⊂N、整数周期 hβ​ 以及有限表
Iβ​:Z/hβ​Z→{0,1},cβ​:Z/hβ​Z→Z,
使得对所有充分大的 m：
Φβ,m​ 单射⟺Iβ​(mmodhβ​)=1,
且在单射情形
ℓcau​(β,m)=m−cβ​(mmodhβ​).
所有异常 aperture、非单射周期碰撞及最优未来解码长度均由定理中的有限表完全列出。

这里“最终周期”作用于 aperture 参数 m，不是整数集合的 ultimate-periodicity problem；二者不得混同。
现有工具能够直接承担的部分
四个最小多项式的有限性可由根的系数界直接得到：若根为
β,α,αˉ，则 1<β<2、∣α∣<1，从而迹、二阶对称和与范数只能取有限多个整数值；逐个根判定后只剩上述四项。四个 finite Parry 字均可用对应多项式恒等式及后缀条件直接验证。Bassino 的三次展开分类可作为已发表的交叉核对来源，而不必重新建立整个三次分类。SciSpace
每个基数的 Qm​ 都满足固定线性递推；现稿的 Toeplitz 系统和碰撞图随后原样适用。
仍缺的最小桥接引理
需要证明一个固定递推的 carry 稳定化定理：

对上述四个 Q-递推，存在一个与 aperture m 无关的有限 carry 图，并允许转移规则依赖 mmodhβ​；所有 sufficiently large m 的差分碰撞图长路径均由该固定图控制。

这不是把完全分类重新写成假设。它把指数级、随 m 变化的 fiber-product 图压缩成固定有限图；一旦建立，最终周期性和精确延迟表由有限图计算得出。
最短证明路线
先完成排名 1 的 1101 基数。随后分别处理：


111：递推最简单，有限枚举显示 m≥4 时延迟恒为 2；


101：三步消元应给出按 mmod3 的终端路径分类；


10001：Parry 多项式分解
x5−x4−1=(x2−x+1)(x3−x−1)
暗示必须保留模 6 的边界相位；这是整个分类中唯一真正困难的部分。


最便宜且有决定性的证伪测试
对四个基数分别构造候选固定 carry 图，而不是继续扩大原始的 3m−1 状态图。若 carry 的绝对值不能获得与 m 无关的上界，或新的状态持续随 m 出现，则“最终有限表”路线应立即放弃。
本轮独立有限检查已经给出一个重要警告：对 x3−x−1 的根，aperture m=6,…,11 单射，但 m=12,13,14 又重新非单射，m=15,16 再次单射。因此任何“达到某个阈值后永久单射”的候选都已被否决；只有保留 aperture 相位的最终周期分类仍与数据相容。

三、综合排序依据
排名 1 的优势在于：桥接引理只有一个固定递推、一个二进制差分字母表和奇偶两类终端状态；有限检验已显示极整齐的精确公式。其成功后还会把现稿的“固定代数次数但变化基数”无界性提升为“固定基数、固定字母表”无界性，概念增量最大且证明风险可控。
排名 2 的潜在影响更广，但其成败取决于 10001 基数的非单调碰撞结构能否压缩为固定相位 carry 图。当前数据支持“复杂但有限”，尚不足以支持最终周期性证明。因此其期望值低于排名 1。
第三项不列入。 向一般 simple-Parry 基数、任意 Parry 基数或任意正则数系扩张时，Parry 多项式的互补因子可具有非 cyclotomic 的复根，当前工具尚不能保证 aperture 参数上的 carry 状态稳定；此时既没有参数一致桥接，也没有比原碰撞图指数界更强的可证目标。列入第三项会违反用户规定的“标准对象桥接、非循环性、参数一致性及自身证伪测试”四项合取。

\subsection{Arithmetic identifiability and optimal local structure}

The threshold classification leaves three finer questions: whether a fixed
aperture identifies the parameter chamber, how many consecutive output
symbols are needed by a future-only inverse, and whether the finite-block and
finite-type bounds in Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} are
sharp.  We answer the first and third completely.  For the future-only
inverse question we
obtain the exact positive-chamber value and the exact negative-chamber value
at aperture three; the parameter-uniform negative-chamber value at larger
apertures remains an explicit interface.

\begin{theorem}[Aperture-two chamber duality and first separation]
\label{thm:quadratic-aperture-two-duality}
Let $q\ge3$ and $2\le\kappa\le q-1$, and let
\begin{align*}
\beta^-_{q,\kappa}&>1,
& (\beta^-_{q,\kappa})^2-(q-1)\beta^-_{q,\kappa}-\kappa&=0,\\
\beta^+_{q,\kappa}&>1,
& (\beta^+_{q,\kappa})^2-q\beta^+_{q,\kappa}+(q-\kappa)&=0.
\end{align*}
After identifying both digit alphabets with $\{0,\ldots,q-1\}$, their
aperture-two legal languages, ranks, folds, and sliding recodings coincide
pointwise.  Explicitly,
\[
Q_0=1,\qquad Q_1=q,\qquad Q_2=q(q-1)+\kappa,
\]
and
\[
X_2=\{(u,v):v<q-1\ \hbox{or}\ u<\kappa\},
\qquad
\Fold_2(u,v)=
\begin{cases}
(u,v),&v<q-1\ \hbox{or}\ u<\kappa,\\
(u-\kappa,0),&v=q-1\ \hbox{and}\ u\ge\kappa.
\end{cases}
\]
Moreover, these are all cross-chamber coincidences at aperture two: the pair
$(q,\kappa)$ is recovered from the digit alphabet and the fold, and its
equivalence class contains the displayed negative- and positive-conjugate
bases when $\kappa\ge2$.  For $\kappa=1$ only the negative-conjugate member
is a quadratic Pisot base in the parameter ranges of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}.

The ambiguity is first resolved at aperture three.  In low-to-high
orientation,
\[
X_3^{(\beta^-_{q,\kappa})}
 \setminus X_3^{(\beta^+_{q,\kappa})}
=\{(h,\kappa-1,q-1):\kappa\le h\le q-1\},
\]
the reverse difference is empty, and
\[
Q_3(\beta^-_{q,\kappa})-Q_3(\beta^+_{q,\kappa})=q-\kappa.
\]
Thus the complete aperture-two input--output map does not determine the sign
of the conjugate, whereas the aperture-three legal language does.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For the negative chamber take $(a,b)=(q-1,\kappa)$; for the positive chamber
take $(a,b)=(q,q-\kappa)$.  The stated parameter bounds are precisely those
which place both choices in Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers}.  In both
cases $d=q-1$, while Proposition~\ref{prop:parry-rank-bijection} gives
\[
Q_2=(q-1)q+\kappa=q^2-(q-\kappa).
\]
The two length-two Parry conditions both say that a high digit $q-1$ must be
followed, in the low-to-high convention, by a digit smaller than $\kappa$.
The displayed fold now follows directly from cyclic reduction of
$u+qv$ modulo $Q_2$.  This proves pointwise equality of the two sliding maps.

Conversely, the alphabet gives $q$, and the least $u$ for which
$(u,q-1)$ is sent to $(0,0)$ gives $\kappa$.  The chamber parametrization in
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-pisot-chambers} then leaves exactly the two displayed
members when $\kappa\ge2$ and only the negative member when $\kappa=1$.

Finally, the two quasi-greedy boundary words are
\[
d_{\beta^-}^*(1)=((q-1)(\kappa-1))^\infty,
\qquad
d_{\beta^+}^*(1)=(q-1)(\kappa-1)^\infty.
\]
Their first two digits agree.  At length three, the negative boundary permits
a suffix $(q-1,\kappa-1,h)$ for every $h\le q-1$, whereas the positive
boundary requires $h\le\kappa-1$.  All other suffix tests agree.  Reversing
to the low-to-high convention gives exactly the stated set difference.
There are $q-\kappa$ such words.  Equivalently, the two recurrences give
\[
Q_3^-=(q-1)Q_2+\kappa q,\qquad
Q_3^+=qQ_2-(q-\kappa)q,
\]
whose difference is $q^2-Q_2=q-\kappa$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Open negative-chamber two-window interface]
\label{rem:negative-two-window-open-interface}
For $a\ge b\ge1$ and $m\ge4$, the natural candidate statement is that two
consecutive congruences
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_j\equiv
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{j+1}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m},
\qquad |e_j|\le a,
\]
force $e_0=0$ for the recurrence
$Q_{j+2}=aQ_{j+1}+bQ_j$.  Finite enumeration supports this assertion, but a
parameter-uniform proof requires an exhaustive bounded-carry invariant.  In
particular, sorting only by the two multiples of $Q_m$ and then appealing to
the signs of successive quotient coefficients does not prove that all carry
branches have been covered.  We therefore do not use or claim this candidate
statement below.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Future-only inverse bounds and the positive-chamber optimum]
\label{thm:quadratic-optimal-causal-inverse}
For $m\ge3$ define
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{\ell\ge1:\ a_t=\psi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+\ell-1})
\text{ for every }y=\Phi_{\beta,m}(a)\}.
\]
Then
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)=3\quad(\beta'>0),
\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,3)=2\quad(\beta'<0).
\]
For $\beta'<0$ and $m\ge4$, the proved bounds are
\[
2\le \ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)\le m.
\]

If, explicitly, $(P)\subset\mathbb Z[z]$ denotes the principal ideal
generated by $P$ and
\[
\mathfrak a(P,d)=\min\{\deg H:0\ne H\in(P),\ H(0)\ne0,
\ \max_j|[z^j]H|\le d\},
\]
then
\[
\mathfrak a(P^-_{a,b},a)=2,
\qquad
\mathfrak a(P^+_{a,b},a-1)=3.
\]
Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}=\mathfrak a$ in the positive chamber for every
$m\ge3$ and in the negative chamber at $m=3$; equality in the negative
chamber for $m\ge4$ is precisely the interface in
Remark~\ref{rem:negative-two-window-open-interface}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose first that $P=P^-_{a,b}$, so $d=a$.  The coefficient vector
$(b,a,-1)$ of $-P$ is the difference of two digit blocks and has nonzero
first coordinate.  Padding it by zeros gives two raw length-$m$ words with
the same single output, so one output never suffices.  Conversely, equality
of two consecutive outputs at $m=3$ gives two overlapping triples from the
complete negative-chamber list in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:quadratic-sliding-congruences}.  A triple in that list with
nonzero first coordinate is necessarily $\pm(b,a,-1)$; the next triple would
then begin with $\pm(a,-1)$, which is not the initial pair of any triple in
the list.  Hence $e_0=0$, and two outputs determine the first digit at
the integral ideal is a nonzero integral multiple of $P^+_{a,b}$ and has
coefficient norm at least $a>d$, while $(1+z)P^+_{a,b}$ has coefficient norm
at most $a-1$.  Hence $\mathfrak a(P^+_{a,b},a-1)=3$, proving the last
assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Critical two-fixed-point normal form]
\label{thm:quadratic-critical-normal-form}
Let $\beta$ be an extremizer in
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}, put
$q=\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1$ and $d=q-1$, and consider its critical map
$\Phi_{\beta,2}$.  Then
\[
\Fold_{\beta,2}(u,v)=
\begin{cases}
(u,v),&(u,v)\ne(d,d),\\
(0,0),&(u,v)=(d,d).
\end{cases}
\]
Its only nonsingleton two-sided fiber is
\[
\Phi_{\beta,2}^{-1}((00)^{\mathbb Z})
=\{0^{\mathbb Z},d^{\mathbb Z}\}.
\]
Consequently
\[
h_{\rm top}(Y_{\beta,2})=\log q,
\qquad
\#\operatorname{Fix}(\sigma^n|_{Y_{\beta,2}})=q^n-1,
\qquad
\zeta_{Y_{\beta,2}}(z)=\frac{1-z}{1-qz}.
\]
The critical image is strictly sofic.  Its right Fischer cover has vertices
$0,\ldots,d$ and one edge $u\to v$ for every ordered pair; the edge label is
$(u,v)$ except that $d\to d$ is labeled $(0,0)$.  The cover is bi-resolving,
and the factor is one-to-one away from the two fixed points above.  Hence, in
the standard terminology, the critical image is a near-Markov AFT shift and
its multiplicity graph is $K_{2,1}$: the two exceptional domain orbits lie
over the single exceptional image orbit.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At either extremal locus the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} has $\kappa=d$, which gives
the displayed rule.  Every nonzero output symbol records its raw edge
uniquely and hence propagates the raw digits uniquely in both directions.
If every output symbol is $(0,0)$, each raw edge is either $0\to0$ or
$d\to d$; overlap forces one of the two constant paths.  This proves the
fiber statement.

The map is finite-to-one from the full $q$-shift, so entropy is $\log q$.
A point fixed by $\sigma^n$ outside the exceptional image has a unique
preimage and that preimage is fixed by $\sigma^n$.  The $q^n$ fixed points
of the full shift therefore map to $q^n-1$ fixed image points, since exactly
the two constant preimages are identified.  The zeta identity follows from
\[
\exp\left(\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{(q^n-1)z^n}{n}\right)
=\frac{1-z}{1-qz}.
\]

The displayed graph is right- and left-resolving and follower-separated.
Every vertex is reached by a one-symbol label different from $(0,0)$, which
is intrinsically synchronizing.  Lemma~\ref{lem:fischer-cover-criterion}
therefore identifies it as the right Fischer cover.

To see that the image is not an SFT, fix $N$ and choose $L>N$.  The word
\[
(d,0)(0,0)^L(d,0)
\]
is not admissible: the first anchor forces the intervening hidden state to be
$0$, while the second requires it to be $d$.  Every subword of length at
most $N+1$ meets at most one anchor and is admissible, using the constant
$0$ or constant $d$ hidden path as appropriate.  Thus no finite forbidden
list defines the image.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact finite-block onset, Fischer cover, and Markov order]
\label{thm:quadratic-exact-local-order}
Let $m\ge m_*(\beta)$ and define
\[
\delta(\beta,m)=\min\{n\ge1:\Phi_{\beta,m,n}\text{ is injective}\}.
\]
Then
\[
\delta(\beta,m)=m.
\]
The natural overlap graph with vertex set
$\mathcal A_\beta^{m-1}$ and edge labels $\Fold_{\beta,m}$ is the right
Fischer cover of $Y_{\beta,m}$ and has exactly
$(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{m-1}$ vertices.  In the output alphabet fixed in
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}, the image is an $m$-step SFT but
not an $(m-1)$-step SFT.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The upper bound $\delta(\beta,m)\le m$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold}.  For $m\ge3$ the reverse
inequality has a uniform certificate.  In the negative chamber, place the
difference vector $(b,a,-1)$ in raw positions $m-2,m-1,m$ and zeros
elsewhere.  In the positive chamber place
$(b,b-a,1-a,1)$ in positions $m-3,\ldots,m$.  Each vector is the difference

 succeeded in 188ms:
\section{Classical pair graphs and simple-Parry arithmetic quotients}
\label{sec:simple-parry-causal-obstructions}

The pair-graph decision used in this section is classical.  Decision
procedures for injectivity and surjectivity of one-dimensional local maps go
back to tessellation automata and cellular automata
\cite{AmorosoPatt1972,Richardson1972}.  Nasu's bundle graphs encode two input
paths with a common image, and Head formulates injectivity as unambiguity of a
labeled finite automaton \cite{Nasu1977,Head1989}.  In symbolic dynamics this
is the familiar fiber-product or pair-graph construction
\cite{LindMarcus1995,Nasu1995}.  A reachable off-diagonal directed cycle,
its periodic repetition, and the existence of some finite sliding-block
inverse in the injective case all belong to that tradition.  Quantitative
decoder windows likewise have an established theory
\cite{Ashley1988,Ashley1991,Ashley1996}.

What is specific here is the quotient of the full input-pair graph by the
coordinatewise difference: for cyclic language-rank recodings it closes on
the finite state set $\Delta_d^{m-1}$.  This arithmetic quotient gives the
exact memory-zero anticipation as a longest survival time, a concrete state
bound, and, for simple-Parry ranks, a unique-zero-predecessor property that
turns a general two-sided inverse into a future-only inverse.  The aperture
trichotomy and the exact quadratic and cubic depths are computations in this
quotient, not new pair-graph principles.  Throughout, the congruences concern
integer language ranks, not equality of numerical $\beta$-values.

Recall that Pisot bases have eventually periodic greedy expansions of one
\cite{Schmidt1980}, whereas the finite expansion required below is the
simple-Parry hypothesis.  The collision results through
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two} do not require the base to be
Pisot.  Parry admissibility and the associated Bertrand numeration are standard
\cite{Parry1960,CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}; the expansions of one for
cubic Pisot numbers were classified by Bassino \cite{Bassino2002}.  We record
the data needed
to distinguish these published inputs from the new sliding-congruence
statement.

Let $\beta\notin\mathbb Z$ be a simple-Parry number with
\[
d_\beta(1)=t_1t_2\cdots t_p0^\infty,
\qquad t_1=d=\lfloor\beta\rfloor,
\qquad t_p>0,
\]
where $p\ge2$ and $0\le t_i\le d$.  The language used below is the canonical
Parry language determined by
$d_\beta^*(1)=(t_1\cdots t_{p-1}(t_p-1))^\infty$, not the noncanonical
Bertrand system generated directly by the finite word $d_\beta(1)$.
Bertrand--Mathis introduced the positional framework
\cite{BertrandMathis1989}; the canonical/noncanonical distinction in the
simple-Parry case is part of the correction and classification in
\cite{CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}.  Put
\[
P_\beta(z)=z^p-t_1z^{p-1}-\cdots-t_p,
\qquad
\mathcal A_d=\{0,1,\ldots,d\},
\qquad
\Delta_d=[-d,d]\cap\mathbb Z.
\]
The raw system is the full shift $\mathcal A_d^{\mathbb Z}$, so $\Delta_d$
is exactly its digit-difference alphabet.  Let $Q_n$ be the number of legal
length-$n$ words.  In the low-to-high convention used throughout this
article, the established Parry--Bertrand rank data are
\begin{align}
Q_0&=1,\nonumber\\
Q_n&=1+\sum_{i=1}^n t_iQ_{n-i} &&(1\le n<p),\label{eq:simple-parry-Q}\\
Q_n&=\sum_{i=1}^p t_iQ_{n-i} &&(n\ge p),\nonumber
\end{align}
and
\[
\Rank_{\beta,m}(w)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}w_jQ_j
\]
maps the legal length-$m$ words, in colexicographic order, bijectively onto
$\{0,\ldots,Q_m-1\}$.  We therefore define $\Fold_{\beta,m}$ and
$\Phi_{\beta,m}$ by the same rank reduction modulo $Q_m$ as in
Definition~\ref{def:quadratic-parry-fold}.

For $m\ge2$, define
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:a_t=\psi(y_t,\ldots,y_{t+L-1})
\text{ for every }y=\Phi_{\beta,m}(a)\},
\label{eq:simple-parry-causal-length}
\end{equation}
with value $\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.  Thus
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)-1$ is the least anticipation of an inverse
sliding-block code constrained to have memory zero.  We do not use
``right-closing delay'' as a synonym: the latter belongs to general
factor-map theory, while \eqref{eq:simple-parry-causal-length} fixes a
one-sided convention and asks for its exact optimum.  Also set
\[
\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)
=\min\{\deg H:0\ne H\in(P_\beta),\ H(0)\ne0,
\ |[z^j]H|\le d\text{ for all }j\}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Degeneration of the bounded-multiple order]
\label{prop:simple-parry-bounded-order}
Under the preceding simple-Parry hypotheses,
\[
\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)=p.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The coefficient vector of $P_\beta$ consists of the leading coefficient
$1$ and the coefficients $-t_1,\ldots,-t_p$.  It lies in $\Delta_d$, and its
constant coefficient $-t_p$ is nonzero.  Hence
$\mathfrak a(P_\beta,d)\le p$.  Conversely, every nonzero member of the
principal ideal $(P_\beta)\subset\mathbb Z[z]$ has degree at least
$\deg P_\beta=p$.  This proves equality.
\end{proof}

For $L\ge1$, introduce the bounded sliding-congruence obstruction
\begin{equation}
\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}
=\left\{e\in\Delta_d^{m+L-1}:
e_0\ne0,\quad
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}
\ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
\label{eq:bad-sliding-congruence-set}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, let $T_{m,L}$ be the $L$-row banded Toeplitz matrix obtained by
placing the kernel $(Q_0,\ldots,Q_{m-1})$ at offsets
$t=0,\ldots,L-1$.  Then the last condition is
$T_{m,L}e\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}$.

\subsection{The classical decision in the arithmetic difference quotient}

\begin{theorem}[Arithmetic quotient of the classical pair-graph decision]
\label{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}
Apply the classical pair-graph construction to the cyclic rank recoding of a
nonintegral simple-Parry number.  For every $m\ge2$, its coordinatewise
difference quotient satisfies
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{\beta,m,L}=\varnothing\},
\label{eq:exact-causal-obstruction}
\end{equation}
where the minimum is $\infty$ when all the obstruction sets are nonempty.

More explicitly, let $\Gamma_{\beta,m}$ be the directed graph with vertex set
$V=\Delta_d^{m-1}$.  There is an edge from
$v=(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})$ to $w=(w_0,\ldots,w_{m-2})$ precisely when
\[
w_i=v_{i+1}\quad(0\le i<m-2)
\]
and
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}Q_jv_j+Q_{m-1}w_{m-2}
\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-edge}
\end{equation}
If $B=\{v\in V:v_0\ne0\}$, then
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
=1+\sup\{\text{number of edges in a directed path starting in }B\}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-longest-path}
\end{equation}
The supremum is infinite exactly when a directed cycle is reachable from
$B$.  Furthermore,
\begin{equation}
\Phi_{\beta,m}\text{ is injective}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Gamma_{\beta,m}\text{ has no bi-infinite path other than the zero path}.
\label{eq:collision-graph-injectivity}
\end{equation}
If $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is injective, then its future-only inverse length is
finite exactly when the zero vertex is not reachable from $B$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $u,v\in\mathcal A_d^{m+L-1}$ and put $e_i=u_i-v_i$.  Since the legal
rank is a bijection, the $t$th output symbols of the two blocks are equal if
and only if their raw ranks agree modulo $Q_m$, namely if and only if
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}Q_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}.
can then occur, because its periodic repetition would be a nonzero
bi-infinite path.  The zero loop is consequently the only cycle reachable
from $B$ that is compatible with injectivity.  Thus paths from $B$ have
unbounded length exactly when they reach the zero vertex.  Combining this
with \eqref{eq:collision-graph-longest-path} proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Simple-Parry consequence of the classical decision]
\label{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}
Under the preceding hypotheses, for every $m\ge2$ the following are
equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is injective;
  \item $\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)<\infty$;
  \item no directed cycle of $\Gamma_{\beta,m}$ is reachable from $B$.
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions hold, then
\begin{equation}
1\le \ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)
\le (2d+1)^{m-1}-1.
\label{eq:simple-parry-causal-state-bound}
\end{equation}
If they fail, there are distinct periodic raw points with the same image and
with common period at most $(2d+1)^{m-1}-1$.  Thus the classical finite
forward cycle search specializes to the arithmetic difference quotient
above.  Every failure has a purely periodic certificate with the displayed
explicit bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The recurrence \eqref{eq:simple-parry-Q} gives $Q_1=d+1$ and shows
inductively that $Q_n>Q_{n-1}$: before the terminal index there is the
additional summand $1$, while at and after that index the positive term
$t_pQ_{n-p}$ accompanies $dQ_{n-1}$.  In particular, $Q_m>d$.

Let $\mathbf0=(0,\ldots,0)\in\Delta_d^{m-1}$.  If an edge
$v\to\mathbf0$ exists, the overlap conditions give
$v_1=\cdots=v_{m-2}=0$, and the edge congruence reduces to
$v_0\equiv0\pmod{Q_m}$.  Since $|v_0|\le d<Q_m$, necessarily
$v=\mathbf0$.  Hence the zero vertex has the zero vertex as its unique
predecessor and is not reachable from $B$.

If $\Phi_{\beta,m}$ is injective, the last assertion of
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision} now gives a finite
future-only inverse.  The converse is immediate because a decoder recovers every raw
coordinate.  A directed cycle reachable from $B$ cannot contain
$\mathbf0$; repeating its first-coordinate labels gives a nonzero periodic
difference sequence and hence, by taking positive and negative parts, two
distinct periodic raw points with the same image.  Conversely, from any
nonzero bi-infinite collision path, shift to a vertex in $B$.  Its forward
path avoids $\mathbf0$ and therefore repeats among the
$(2d+1)^{m-1}-1$ nonzero vertices, producing a reachable directed cycle.
This proves all three equivalences and the periodic certificate, whose cycle
may be chosen simple.

In the injective case the forward subgraph reachable from $B$ is acyclic and
contains at most $(2d+1)^{m-1}-1$ vertices.  A directed path in it has at
most $(2d+1)^{m-1}-2$ edges.  Formula
\eqref{eq:collision-graph-longest-path} then gives
\eqref{eq:simple-parry-causal-state-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete aperture-two trichotomy]
\label{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two}
Set
\[
c_\beta=t_2+\ind_{\{p\ge3\}}.
\]
Then $1\le c_\beta\le d+1$ and
\[
Q_2=d(d+1)+c_\beta.
\]
Exactly one of the following alternatives holds.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
  \item If $c_\beta=d+1$, then $\Fold_{\beta,2}$ is the identity on
  $\mathcal A_d^2$ and $\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,2)=1$.
  \item If $1\le c_\beta<d$, then $\Phi_{\beta,2}$ is injective and
  $\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,2)=2$.
  \item If $c_\beta=d$, then $\Phi_{\beta,2}$ is noninjective.  Its sole
  nonsingleton path-space fiber is
  \[
  \Phi_{\beta,2}(0^{\mathbb Z})
  =\Phi_{\beta,2}(d^{\mathbb Z})
  =((0,0))^{\mathbb Z},
  \]
  and $\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,2)=\infty$.
\end{enumerate}
For $p=2$ the three cases are determined by $c_\beta=t_2$; for
$p\ge3$ they are determined by $c_\beta=t_2+1$.  In particular, a binary
simple-Parry word of order at least three has an identity aperture-two fold
when $t_2=1$ and exactly the displayed constant branch pair when $t_2=0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Put $q=d+1$ and $M=Q_2=dq+c_\beta$.  The formula for $Q_2$ follows directly
from \eqref{eq:simple-parry-Q}, with the boundary summand $1$ present exactly
when $2<p$.  Since $t_2\ge1$ when $p=2$, whereas $0\le t_2\le d$ when
$p\ge3$, the stated range for $c_\beta$ follows.

An edge of $\Gamma_{\beta,2}$ is a pair $(e,f)\in\Delta_d^2$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
e+qf\equiv0\pmod M.
\label{eq:aperture-two-edge-congruence}
\end{equation}
The left side has modulus at most
$d(1+q)=q^2-1<2M$, so it equals $-M$, $0$, or $M$.  If it is zero, then
$q>|e|$ forces $(e,f)=(0,0)$.  If it is $M$, the bound $e\le d$ forces
$f=d$, because $f\le d-1$ would give
$M-qf\ge q+c_\beta>d$; hence $(e,f)=(c_\beta,d)$, which is admissible
exactly when $c_\beta\le d$.  The case $-M$ is its negative.  Thus the full
edge set is
\[
\{(0,0)\}\quad(c_\beta=d+1),
\qquad
\{(0,0),\ \pm(c_\beta,d)\}\quad(c_\beta\le d).
\]

When $c_\beta=d+1$, one has $Q_2=q^2=|\mathcal A_d^2|$, so every raw
length-two word is legal and cyclic reduction does nothing.  This proves
(a).  If $c_\beta<d$, the two nonzero edges cannot be followed by another
edge, because their terminal vertices are $\pm d$, not $\pm c_\beta$.
There is a one-output collision but no two-output collision, so
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision} gives (b).  Finally, when
$c_\beta=d$, the two nonzero edges are the loops $(d,d)$ and $(-d,-d)$.
They give precisely the constant difference sequences $\pm d^{\mathbb Z}$.
The only raw realization of either is the pair
$\{0^{\mathbb Z},d^{\mathbb Z}\}$, and the common residue is zero in every
window.  No other nonzero bi-infinite path exists, proving (c) and the fiber
classification.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Pisot-free scope is strict]
\label{prop:nonpisot-simple-parry-scope}
Proposition~\ref{prop:simple-parry-bounded-order},
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}, Corollary
\ref{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}, and Theorem
\ref{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two} hold for every nonintegral simple-Parry
number; no Pisot hypothesis is required.  This strictly enlarges their scope.
Indeed, let $\beta$ be the positive root of
\begin{equation}
x^4-2x^3-2=0.
\label{eq:nonpisot-simple-parry-witness}
\end{equation}
Then
\[
d_\beta(1)=2002\,0^\infty,
\]
$\beta$ is not Pisot, and the aperture-two cyclic rank fold is injective with
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,2)=2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The recurrence for $Q_n$, the consecutive colex-rank formula, and Parry
admissibility used above hold for every simple-Parry number
\cite{Parry1960,CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}.  The proofs of the four
listed results subsequently use only these data, the full raw alphabet

 succeeded in 171ms:
We next show that, even in algebraic degree three, the future-only inverse lengths at
injective apertures have no uniform bound.  Bassino's classification
\cite{Bassino2002} contains the cubic Pisot family and its finite expansion
of one used below.  We include a direct verification of those input data so
that the new part of the argument is visibly confined to the sliding
collision depth.

For $n\ge4$, put
\begin{equation}
M_n(x)=x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial}
\end{equation}
and define the word
\begin{equation}
w_n=n\,1\,2\cdots(n-2)\,n\,1\,0\,
(n-1)(n-2)\cdots2\,0\,n.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-word}
\end{equation}
Thus $|w_n|=2n+2$.

\begin{lemma}[Bassino's cubic family: direct verification of the required data]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data}
The polynomial $M_n$ has a unique real root $\beta_n$, this root belongs to
$(n,n+1)$, and $\beta_n$ is a nonintegral cubic Pisot number.  Moreover,
\[
d_{\beta_n}(1)=w_n0^\infty.
\]
If $(Q_j)$ denotes its Parry--Bertrand language counts, then, for
$0\le r\le n-1$,
\begin{align}
Q_0&=1,\qquad Q_1=n+1,\qquad Q_2=n^2+n+2,
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}\\
Q_r&=(n+2)Q_{r-1}-2nQ_{r-2}+nQ_{r-3}
\qquad(3\le r\le n-1).
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}
\end{align}
In particular $Q_{j+1}>nQ_j$ throughout this range.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One has
\[
M_n(n)=-n<0,
\qquad
M_n(n+1)=n^2-n-1>0.
\]
The identity
\[
M_n(x)=x^2(x-2)-n(x-1)^2
\]
shows that $M_n(x)<0$ for $x\le n$.  Indeed this is immediate for
$x\le2$, while for $2<x\le n$ one uses
\[
\frac{x^2(x-2)}{(x-1)^2}
=x-\frac{x}{(x-1)^2}<x\le n.
\]
If all three roots were real, every root other than the one in $(n,n+1)$
would also have to exceed $n$; their sum could then not equal $n+2$.
Consequently $\beta_n$ is the unique real root.  A reducible monic cubic
over $\mathbb Z$ has an integral root, which is impossible because the only
real root lies strictly between consecutive integers.  Thus $M_n$ is
irreducible.  Its two remaining roots are conjugate, and their common
modulus is
\[
\sqrt{\frac{n}{\beta_n}}<1
\]
by the product of the roots.  This proves the asserted Pisot and degree
statements.

Let $P_{w_n}(x)=x^{2n+2}-\sum_{i=1}^{2n+2}(w_n)_i
x^{2n+2-i}$ be the Parry polynomial of the displayed word.  Direct
multiplication gives the polynomial identity
\begin{equation}
P_{w_n}(x)
=M_n(x)\frac{(x^n-1)(x^{n+1}-1)}{(x-1)^2}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-parry-factorization}
\end{equation}
Hence $w_n$ represents one in base $\beta_n$.  The digit $n$ occurs only
at the first, the $n$th, and the last positions.  The suffix beginning at
the $n$th position starts with $n10$, whereas $w_n$ starts with $n12$;
the last suffix is $n0^\infty$.  Every other proper suffix starts with a
digit below $n$.  All proper suffixes are therefore lexicographically
smaller than $w_n0^\infty$, so Parry's criterion gives the claimed greedy
expansion.

For $r\le n-1$, its first $r$ digits are $t_1=n$ and $t_i=i-1$ for
$2\le i\le r$.  Formula~\eqref{eq:simple-parry-Q} becomes
\begin{equation}
Q_r=1+nQ_{r-1}+\sum_{i=2}^r(i-1)Q_{r-i}.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q}
\end{equation}
This gives \eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-initial-Q}, proves $Q_r>nQ_{r-1}$,
and, by subtracting the formula at the preceding three indices, gives
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-Q-recurrence}.
\end{proof}

For the next lemma write $S_k=\sum_{j=0}^kQ_j$ and
$D_k=Q_k-nQ_{k-1}$.  Formula
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-positive-Q} and the cubic recurrence give,
respectively,
\begin{align}
D_k&=1+\sum_{i=2}^k(i-1)Q_{k-i},
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-positive}\\
D_k&=2Q_{k-2}-(n-2)Q_{k-3}+2S_{k-4}
\qquad(k\ge3),
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-D-alternate}
\end{align}
where $S_{-1}=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Parameter-uniform two-window carry exclusion]
\label{lem:cubic-unbounded-two-window}
\]
The last original window consequently reads
$Q_{r-1}e_{2r-2}=0$, so $e_{2r-2}=0$.  The first window then reads
$e_0=c_0Q_r$.  Since $|e_0|\le n<Q_r$, it follows that
$e_0=c_0=0$.  Thus $\mathcal K_{n,r,r}=\{0\}$, with every carry and
coordinate exhausted.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Bassino family: unbounded future-only inverse length]
\label{thm:cubic-unbounded-causal-depth}
For Bassino's family, let $n\ge4$, let $\beta_n\in(n,n+1)$ be the root of
\eqref{eq:cubic-unbounded-polynomial} and put $m_n=n-1$.  Then $\beta_n$
is a nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot number, $\Phi_{\beta_n,m_n}$ is
injective, and
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=n-1.
\label{eq:cubic-unbounded-causal-length}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\sup\left\{\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m):
\substack{\beta\text{ nonintegral cubic simple-Parry Pisot},\\
m\ge2,\ \Phi_{\beta,m}\text{ injective}}\right\}=\infty.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-family-data} supplies all base and language
hypotheses.  At $r=m_n$, Lemma
\ref{lem:cubic-unbounded-terminal-paths} gives
\[
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r-1}=\{E_{n,r},-E_{n,r}\},
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\beta_n,r,r}=\varnothing.
\]
The exact collision criterion, Theorem
\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision}, now gives
$\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=m_n=n-1$.  Its finiteness implies
injectivity by Corollary~\ref{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness}, and
letting $n$ tend to infinity proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change â€” all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** â€” check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) â€” this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** â€” one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** â€” with trade-offs and your recommendation
5. **Present design** â€” in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
6. **Write design doc** â€” save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
7. **Spec self-review** â€” quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
8. **User reviews written spec** â€” ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
9. **Transition to implementation** â€” invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan

## Process Flow

```dot

**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.

## The Process

**Understanding the idea:**

- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec â†’ plan â†’ implementation cycle.
- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria

**Exploring approaches:**

- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
- Lead with your recommended option and explain why

**Presenting the design:**

- Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense

**Design for isolation and clarity:**

- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.

**Working in existing codebases:**

- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
- Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.

## After the Design

**Documentation:**

- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
- Commit the design document to git

**Spec Self-Review:**
After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:

1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.

Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review â€” just fix and move on.

**User Review Gate:**
After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:

> "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."

Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.

**Implementation:**

- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.

## Key Principles

- **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense

## Visual Companion

A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool â€” not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.

**Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:
> "Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)"

**This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.

**Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**

- **Use the browser** for content that IS visual â€” mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs
- **Use the terminal** for content that is text â€” requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions

A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question â€” use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question â€” use the browser.

If they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:
`skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`

\title{Overlap thresholds and unbounded future-only inverse depth for cyclic ranks of
Pisot $\boldsymbol{\beta}$-languages}

\author{Haobo Ma$^{1}$ and Wenlin Zhang$^{2,*}$\\[4pt]
{\small $^1$AELF Pte. Ltd., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1,}\\

\begin{abstract}
We study the symbolic dynamics of a cyclic recoding of greedy
$\beta$-languages.  The canonical Parry--Bertrand rank of a legal length-$m$
word is extended to the full digit cube, reduced modulo the number
$Q_m(\beta)$ of legal words, and overlapped along consecutive windows.  This
is a congruence of integer language ranks, not numerical
$\beta$-normalization in $\mathbb Z[\beta]$.  Our first main result is a full
quadratic Pisot classification: the least nontrivial aperture at which the
sliding recoding is a conjugacy onto its finite-type image is
\[
m_*(\beta)=3
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{minpoly}(\beta)\in
\{x^2-ax-a:a\ge1\}\cup\{x^2-ax+1:a\ge3\},
\]
and $m_*(\beta)=2$ otherwise.  At and above threshold, finite-block
injectivity begins exactly at block length $m$, the natural overlap graph is
the right Fischer cover with $(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{m-1}$ states, and the
image is exactly $m$-step, not $(m-1)$-step, in the fixed output alphabet.
In particular,
$|\mathcal L_N(Y_{\beta,m})|=(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{N+m-1}$ for $N\ge m$.

Our second main result shows that memory-zero inverse anticipation is already
unbounded in algebraic degree three.  For every $n\ge4$, let
$\beta_n\in(n,n+1)$ be the root of
$x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n$ and set $m_n=n-1$.  Then $\beta_n$ is a nonintegral
cubic simple-Parry Pisot number, the aperture-$m_n$ recoding is injective,
and
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=n-1,
\]
so the optimal inverse anticipation is $n-2$.  This is Bassino's cubic
simple-Parry family; the new assertion is the exact depth of its
rank-modulo-$Q_m$ sliding recoding.

The mechanism is an arithmetic quotient of the classical pair/fiber graph
for one-dimensional local codes: input pairs collapse to difference states
in $[-\lfloor\beta\rfloor,\lfloor\beta\rfloor]^{m-1}$.  In the simple-Parry
setting this quotient computes the exact future-only inverse length by a
longest-path formula, strengthens invertibility to a memory-zero inverse via
the unique predecessor of the zero state, and gives explicit bounded
periodic failure certificates.  Its aperture-two trichotomy and the
quadratic/cubic exact depths are the map-specific arithmetic conclusions.
\end{abstract}

\medskip
\noindent{\bf 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification.}
Primary: 37B10, 37B40; Secondary: 11B39, 37A35, 11A67, 11K38

\medskip
\noindent{\bf Key words and phrases.}
Pisot number, beta-language, ordered-language rank, cyclic recoding, symbolic dynamics, future-only inverse, collision graph

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}

Finite-window factor codes can collapse individual words and nevertheless
become invertible when successive windows overlap.  For subshifts generated
by $\beta$-expansions, this raises a concrete classification problem: how many
overlapping cyclic rank windows are needed before the full digit shift is
recovered, and how much future output is required by an inverse with memory
zero?  We answer the threshold problem for every quadratic Pisot base and
show that the optimal future depth is already unbounded in algebraic degree
three.  The resulting statements concern finite-type images, Fischer covers,
follower separation, and exact Markov order, which are the natural symbolic
dynamical invariants of the construction.

Fix a greedy $\beta$-language and let $Q_m(\beta)$ be the number of its legal
words of length $m$.  Reverse those words, order them lexicographically, and
identify them with $\{0,\ldots,Q_m(\beta)-1\}$.  Equivalently, order the
unreversed words colexicographically.  Evaluate the same positional rank on
the full digit cube, reduce it modulo $Q_m(\beta)$, return the legal word with
that residue, and overlap the resulting windows along the shift.  We call
this map a \emph{cyclic language-rank recoding}.

The positional rank is established numeration theory.  Bertrand--Mathis,
Bruy\`ere--Hansel, and Lecomte--Rigo develop nonintegral-base and
ordered-language numeration \cite{BertrandMathis1989,BruyereHansel1997,
LecomteRigo2001}; Parry's admissibility theorem supplies the canonical greedy
language \cite{Parry1960}.  Charlier--Cisternino--Stipulanti correct the
classification of Bertrand systems and distinguish, for a simple-Parry base,
the canonical system determined by $d_\beta^*(1)$ from the noncanonical
system generated directly by the finite word $d_\beta(1)$
\cite{CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}.  We always use the canonical
language.  Proposition~\ref{prop:parry-rank-bijection} gives a self-contained
two-chamber interval proof of this standard positional rank because its exact
endpoints enter the later estimates.  The map-specific operation begins only
with cyclic reduction of the fixed-length rank and sliding overlap.

This distinction also separates the construction from numerical
$\beta$-normalization.  The congruence is a congruence of integer language
ranks, not of values in $\mathbb Z[\beta]$.  For instance, if
$\beta^2-3\beta+1=0$, then $(Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,3,8)$ and the rank of $(2,2)$ is
$2+2Q_1=8$.  Its cyclic recoding is therefore $(0,0)$, but no equality
$2+2\beta=0$ is asserted.  Finite-transducer normalization results provide
important context while studying a different map
\cite{Frougny1992,FrougnySolomyak1992,BerendFrougny1994,FrougnySteiner2008}.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Quadratic classification.}
Every quadratic Pisot minimal polynomial belongs to one of two chambers,
\[
P^-_{a,b}(x)=x^2-ax-b,\quad a\ge b\ge1,
\qquad
P^+_{a,b}(x)=x^2-ax+b,\quad a\ge3,\quad 1\le b\le a-2.
\]
Let $m_*(\beta)$ be the least aperture $m\ge2$ for which the sliding cyclic
rank recoding is a topological conjugacy from the full digit shift onto its
finite-type image.  Theorem~\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} gives the
complete classification
\[
m_*(\beta)=3
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{minpoly}(\beta)\in
\{x^2-ax-a:a\ge1\}\cup\{x^2-ax+1:a\ge3\},
\]
with $m_*(\beta)=2$ otherwise.  To the authors' knowledge, the exact
threshold for this cyclic fixed-length language-rank recoding has not
previously been determined.  The claim is deliberately restricted to this
map and is not a claim about $\beta$-normalization in general.

At every aperture at or above threshold,
\[
|\mathcal L_N(Y_{\beta,m})|
=(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{N+m-1}\qquad(N\ge m).
\]
The proof rests on a parameter-uniform nearest-multiple separation in both
quadratic chambers and the resulting bounded sliding-congruence lemma.  It
also identifies the exact extremal bases rather than only producing an upper
bound for the aperture.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Exact local structure.}
The threshold has a sharp finite-block and presentation-theoretic form.
Theorem~\ref{thm:quadratic-exact-local-order} proves simultaneously that
finite-block injectivity first occurs at block length $N=m$, that the natural
overlap presentation is the right Fischer cover with exactly
$(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{m-1}$ states, and that the image in its fixed output
alphabet is $m$-step but not $(m-1)$-step.  These conclusions use standard
Fischer--Krieger and resolving-cover theory
\cite{Fischer1975,Krieger1984,LindMarcus1995}; the new content is the exact
identification and order for this arithmetic family.  Theorem
\ref{thm:quadratic-aperture-two-duality} gives the associated cross-chamber
compression at aperture two, and Theorem
\ref{thm:quadratic-critical-normal-form} identifies the unique two-point
fiber and its strictly sofic normal form at the critical parameters.

The Fibonacci and boundary-modified metallic cases provide the prototype.
At the golden base the three-window decoder is explicit, while at aperture
two the two constant raw sequences form the sole nontrivial path-space fiber.
For the constant-type family
\[
\beta_A=\frac{A+\sqrt{A^2+4}}2,
\qquad Q_{j+2}=AQ_{j+1}+Q_j,
\]
the threshold is three only for $A=1$ and is two for every $A\ge2$.  The
weights and boundary convention differ from standard Ostrowski numeration;
that relationship is recorded without identifying the two systems.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Degree-three unboundedness.}
For an injective recoding define $\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta,m)-1$ to be the least
anticipation of an inverse sliding-block code with memory zero.  This is a
directional optimum, not a generic name for right-closing delay.  For every
$n\ge4$, let $\beta_n\in(n,n+1)$ be the real root of
\[
x^3-(n+2)x^2+2nx-n
\]
and put $m_n=n-1$.  Bassino established this cubic simple-Parry Pisot family
and its expansion of one \cite{Bassino2002}.  Theorem
\ref{thm:cubic-unbounded-causal-depth} proves the new map-specific assertion
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\beta_n,m_n)=n-1.
\]
Thus exact memory-zero inverse anticipation is unbounded already within
algebraic degree three.  A parameter-uniform two-window carry exclusion and
an exhaustive terminal-path induction provide the lower and upper bounds.

The proof uses a classical decision in a specialized form.  Pair graphs,
bundle graphs, and fiber products encode pairs of input paths with the same
output; reachable off-diagonal cycles decide noninjectivity and yield
periodic witnesses.  This tradition includes tessellation automata,
one-dimensional cellular automata, and symbolic factor maps
\cite{AmorosoPatt1972,Richardson1972,Nasu1977,Head1989,LindMarcus1995,
Nasu1995}.  Finite decoder-window bounds and resolving maps are likewise
classical \cite{Ashley1988,Ashley1991,Ashley1996}.  Theorem
\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision} applies that decision after an exact
arithmetic quotient: an input pair is replaced by its coordinatewise
difference in $[-d,d]^{m-1}$.  For cyclic Parry ranks this quotient retains
all collisions, computes $\ell_{\rm cau}$ by a longest-path formula, and has
an explicit state bound.  Corollary
\ref{cor:simple-parry-causal-completeness} uses the unique predecessor of the
zero difference state to strengthen the general inverse conclusion to a
future-only inverse.  Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-aperture-two} then gives
the exact aperture-two trichotomy.  These arithmetic quotient and depth
computations are the contribution; the pair-graph/cycle decision itself is
not.

The three headline results therefore form a single dynamical narrative.  The
quadratic theorem classifies every Pisot base in degree two and fixes its
finite-type onset and canonical right-resolving presentation.  The
simple-Parry difference quotient isolates the arithmetic responsible for
future decoding.  Bassino's cubic family then shows that the uniform
quadratic behavior breaks immediately in degree three.

Section~\ref{sec:stabilization} defines the Fibonacci rank recoding, and the
following sections establish its decoder and critical branch locus.  Section
\ref{sec:metallic-cover} treats the metallic prototype.  Section
\ref{sec:quadratic-pisot-extension} proves the full quadratic classification
and exact local structure.  Section
\ref{sec:simple-parry-causal-obstructions} first separates the classical
pair-graph input from the arithmetic difference quotient and then proves the
cubic unbounded-depth theorem.  Section~\ref{sec:discussion} records the
remaining map-specific interfaces.  Entropy, Blackwell-equivalence,
pressure, Fourier fiber statistics, discrepancy, and geometric consequences
are kept outside the main proof chain in the supplementary material.

$$H(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{r}h_jz^j\in(R),\qquad

è‹¥  $\alpha$  æ˜¯  $R$  çš„æ ¹ï¼Œ $0<|\alpha|=s<1$ ï¼Œåˆ™

$$1\le |h_0|

å› æ­¤ï¼š

$$H(z)\longmapsto H(-z)$$

æŠŠæ ¹  $s$  å˜ä¸º  $-s$ ï¼Œå¹¶å°†ç³»æ•°  $h_j$  å˜ä¸º  $(-1)^jh_j$ ï¼Œä¿æŒæ¬¡æ•°ã€å¸¸æ•°é¡¹åŠå¯¹ç§°å·®ç•Œ  $[-d,d]$  ä¸å˜ã€‚å› æ­¤ï¼Œå•ä¸ªå…±è½­æ ¹çš„æ­£è´Ÿå·ä¸èƒ½è§£é‡Š  $\mathfrak a$  çš„å˜åŒ–ï¼›çœŸæ­£ç›¸å…³çš„æ˜¯æ‰€æœ‰æ ¹å…±äº«åŒä¸€æ•´æ•°ç³»æ•°å‘é‡æ‰€äº§ç”Ÿçš„è”åˆçº¦æŸã€‚

3. Concrete mathematical repairs
B1: Correct the mathematical interpretation
For the positive-conjugate example

$$\beta^2-3\beta+1=0,\qquad d=\lfloor\beta\rfloor=2,\qquad
(Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,3,8),$$

the manuscriptâ€™s rank gives

$$\operatorname{Rank}_{\beta,2}(2,2)=2+2Q_1=8\equiv0\pmod{Q_2},$$

so  $\operatorname{Fold}_{\beta,2}(2,2)=(0,0)$ . No result establishes a corresponding congruence

$$2+2\beta\equiv0$$

in a natural quotient of  $\mathbb Z[\beta]$ . The operation is therefore a cyclic congruence of language ranks.
A defensible title would be:

â€œOverlap-injectivity thresholds for cyclic ranks of quadratic Pisot  $\beta$ -languages.â€

The abstract should say that the paper defines a new recoding; it should not imply classification of established Pisot normalization transducers.
B2: Complete Proposition 5.2 by coupled induction
In the positive chamber put

$$d=a-1,\qquad c=a-b-1.$$

Define

$$B_m=\left\{x\in X_m^{(\beta)}:
(x_m,\ldots,x_1)\le_{\rm lex}c^m\right\},
\qquad R_m=|B_m|.$$

Prove simultaneously:

$$\operatorname{Rank}(X_m)=\{0,\ldots,Q_m-1\},
\qquad
\operatorname{Rank}(B_m)=\{0,\ldots,R_m-1\}.$$

The high digit decomposition gives

$$Q_m=dQ_{m-1}+R_{m-1},
\qquad
R_m=cQ_{m-1}+R_{m-1}.$$

Since

$$R_{m-1}=Q_{m-1}-(d-c)Q_{m-2}
       =Q_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2},$$

one obtains

$$Q_m=(d+1)Q_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2}
   =aQ_{m-1}-bQ_{m-2}.$$

For the rank induction, digits  $0,\ldots,d-1$  produce consecutive intervals

$$[hQ_{m-1},(h+1)Q_{m-1}-1],$$

while high digit  $d$  contributes

$$[dQ_{m-1},dQ_{m-1}+R_{m-1}-1].$$

The analogous decomposition with high digits  $0,\ldots,c$  proves the  $B_m$  invariant. This supplies the missing bijectivity, count recurrence, and order preservation.
B3: Replace the unsupported separation paragraph
Let

$$R_r=\frac{Q_{r-1}}{Q_{r-2}}.$$

First establish from the recurrence:

$$a+\frac{b}{a+1}\le R_r<a+\frac ba
\quad(s=+1),$$

and

$$a-\frac{b}{a-1}<R_r\le a-\frac ba
\quad(s=-1),$$

for the relevant indices.
If  $k$  is a nearest integer to  $beQ_{r-1}/Q_r$ , show explicitly that  $0\le k\le b$ . With

$$h=be-ak,$$

division by  $Q_{r-2}$  reduces the desired estimate exactly to

$$|hR_r-kb|\ge1 \quad(s=+1),$$

or

$$|hR_r+kb|\ge1 \quad(s=-1),$$

for

$$1\le e\le d,\qquad 0\le k\le b.$$

The proof must then split according to the sign of  $h$ ; the noncancelling signs are immediate, while the cancelling signs require the ratio bounds and the integral relation  $h=be-ak$ . These inequalitiesâ€”not the current informal â€œdivisionâ€â€”are the actual finite arithmetic lemma needed to infer

$$\operatorname{dist}(beQ_{r-1},Q_r\mathbb Z)\ge Q_{r-2}.$$

If the authors cannot prove these two reduced inequalities uniformly, Theorem 5.4 must be restricted to the parameter range actually verified.
B4: Supply the missing  $m=2$  block decoder
Let  $d>\kappa$ , and write

$$y_i=(p_i,q_i)
 =\operatorname{Fold}_{\beta,2}(a_i,a_{i+1}).$$

From  $y_0,y_1$ , recover  $a_1$  by

$$a_1=
\begin{cases}
q_0,&q_0>0,\\
0,&q_0=0,\ p_1=0,\\
d,&q_0=0,\ p_1\in\{d-\kappa,d\}.
\end{cases}$$

Then recover  $a_0$ :

$$a_0=
\begin{cases}
p_0,&q_0>0,\\
p_0,&q_0=0,\ a_1=0,\\
p_0+\kappa,&q_0=0,\ a_1=d.
\end{cases}$$

Finally recover  $a_2$ :

$$a_2=
\begin{cases}
q_1,&q_1>0,\\
0,&q_1=0,\ p_1=a_1,\\
d,&q_1=0,\ p_1=a_1-\kappa.
\end{cases}$$

The last two alternatives are disjoint because  $\kappa>0$ . Thus

$$\Phi_{\beta,2,2}:A_\beta^3\longrightarrow
\bigl(X_2^{(\beta)}\bigr)^2$$

is injective. Consecutive two-output blocks then cover every raw block, proving injectivity for all  $n\ge2$  and

$$|L_n(Y_{\beta,2})|=(d+1)^{n+1}.$$

B5: Correct Corollary C.2
The claimed superadditivity already fails for simple stationary Markov laws. Let  $P$  be i.i.d. with marginal  $(3/4,1/4)$ , and let  $Q$  be the stationary Markov chain with

$$T_Q=
\begin{pmatrix}
1/2&1/2\\
1/4&3/4
\end{pmatrix},
\qquad \pi_Q=(1/3,2/3).$$

Then

$$D(P_1\Vert Q_1)\approx0.362990,
\qquad
D(P_2\Vert Q_2)\approx0.598426
 <2D(P_1\Vert Q_1).$$

The universally valid conclusion from the manuscriptâ€™s exact block identity is instead

$$\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac1N
D(\eta_{[0,N-1]}\Vert\xi_{[0,N-1]})
=
\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{d_k}{k},$$

and the same equality for  $\liminf$ . This follows directly from

$$D(\eta_{[0,N-1]}\Vert\xi_{[0,N-1]})=d_{N+m-1}$$

and  $(N+m-1)/N\to1$ .
If an actual limit is desired, assume that the reference law  $\nu$  is a stationary finite-order Markov law with stationary transitions. Gray explicitly treats the general rate using a limsup and gives the Markov-reference condition under which the limit exists: R. M. Gray, Entropy and Information Theory.
Medium-issue repairs


M1: Replace â€œConstant-type Ostrowski originâ€ with â€œBoundary-modified constant-type recurrence model.â€ Standard Ostrowski digits satisfy  $b_1<a_1$  and use  $q_k$ , as stated explicitly by Hieronymiâ€“Terry.


M2: Add: â€œThe  $2^{m-1}$ -state conclusion is relative to the stabilized output alphabet  $X_m$ ; Fischer-cover state counts are not invariants of arbitrary topological conjugacy.â€


M4: Include the worked cases

$$x^2-2x-2,\quad x^2-3x-2,\quad x^2-3x+1,\quad x^2-4x+2.$$

These respectively exhibit negative extremal, negative nonextremal, positive extremal, and positive nonextremal behavior.


M5: Archive code with a DOI and report whether tests cover, for example,
 $1\le a\le A_{\max}$ ,  $1\le b\le a$ , and  $m,n\le M_{\max}$ . Finite testing must not be described as an independent certificate of an infinite theorem.


M6: Remove Theorems 3.14 and 4.9 as theorem-level repetitions. Move Appendices A, B, C, E, and F to supplementary material unless required by the chosen venue.

5. Acceptance conditions
Acceptance would require all of the following:


A complete proof of Proposition 5.2 in both chambers.


A valid uniform proof of the separation estimate (5.5).


The missing  $m=2$  finite-block proof for Theorem 5.4.


Correction of Corollary C.2 to limsup/liminf, or addition of sufficient Markov hypotheses.


Explicit reframing of the construction as a cyclic language-rank recoding, not standard  $\beta$ -normalization.


A genuine comparison with abstract/regular-language numeration and classical  $\beta$ -shift literature.


Worked nonmetallic examples and a persistent computational archive.


Substantial compression of standard auxiliary material.


If these points are addressed, the paper could become a strong specialized contribution in symbolic dynamics and numeration. Algebraic patching alone would not suffice: the constructionâ€™s naturality and relation to existing ordered-language numeration must also be made convincing.
